SKAN · SKAN-CH · the new Memo conveyor's card of 19 November 2026

What the Investment Analyst wrote behind each of the eleven ratings: SKAN

The Investment Analyst's answers to the 101 questions and its 13 group judgements, 44,854 words, set out under the eleven ratings: each rating, then each of its lettered sub-items, then the answers beneath.

Word for word. Every answer is the Investment Analyst's text exactly as written; a script checked that every word is on this page, in order. What is changed is layout only: answers are grouped by rating rather than by layer; each answer is titled with the question as it was set (the Investment Analyst's own heading appears beneath only where it adds words); an answer that belongs under more than one sub-item is shown in full once, at its first place, and as a one-line link everywhere else.

Which answer sits where comes from the same table the ratings map uses. Each answer carries its layer and group, because "positive" means something different in each layer. Each layer's own material (what it was asked, what its scores mean, the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices) is at the end, so nothing is left out.

The ratings alone: the ratings map.

How to read the scores and letters

The Investment Analyst ended each answer with a score from 1 to 100: 100 is the most positive for an investment case, 1 the most negative, and 50 neither. What "positive" refers to differs by layer, so each layer states its own meaning (listed just below).

Where the Rater has scored this stock (every memo from 3 November 2026), the number in each pill is the Rater's, set after the memo was written from the Investment Analyst's judgements against a key per group of questions; the Investment Analyst's own score is kept in the pill's tooltip. The letter beside each score is read from the one canonical source for ratings, the same file the eleven ratings page and the memo scorecards read; nothing is graded on this page. The letter follows the item's rule: for most items this stock's rank among the stocks scored on the same scale on that same question or group judgement, re-cut each night (Richard's ruling of 4-Nov-26: every letter by rank; in the file's words: A above 90%; B+ above 82.5% to 90%; B above 75% to 82.5%; C+ above 66.67% to 75%; C above 58.33% to 66.67%; C- above 50% to 58.33%; D above 25% to 50%; F up to 25%); on the External change forces and Internal change forces items the number carries a sign (50 = neither positive nor negative) and the letter is the rank, so read the two together. “37th of 56” is the stock's place among them; a dashed outline marks a provisional grade (fewer than 30 stocks scored).

What the scores mean in each layer

Layer 1

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

Layer 2

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

Layer 3 and Layer 4

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

1

External headwinds/tailwinds

–not yet rated

Memo topic 1, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

External change forces

Layer 1 · External change forces

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group. The rating above stands for it, so it carries no number of its own.

The environment gives SKAN volume, not margin. Regulation (Annex 1), complex oncology and ADC lines and onshoring are forcing demand into exactly SKAN's product, and the order book proves it: a record backlog of 442.3 covering 1.22x trailing sales. But the same environment is shifting bargaining power away from the standalone isolator maker toward turnkey line builders and toward the aftermarket, introducing price-conscious buyers and low-cost entrants, and raising the cost of SKAN's Swiss labour in foreign-currency terms. For the model: drive 2026 to 2027 revenue from backlog conversion with confidence, treat 2028 orders as exposed to a capex plateau, put no real price into equipment, and build an explicit franc line. The external picture supports the revenue line of consensus (sales +19%, +15%, +16%) better than its margin line.

1a

External headwinds/threats

6 answers
C-0234C-0545C-0740C-0946C-3544C-3957
C-02

To what extent, and in which direction, is competitive intensity changing?

F3436th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Competitive intensity: changing, and in which direction?

Judgement: Competitive intensity is rising, structurally and on price, in SKAN's largest product line (process isolators on fill-finish lines). The post-pandemic seller's market has become what the CEO calls an "equilibrium" in which SKAN "must fight much more to win" tenders, and the main threat comes from the filling-machine makers (Syntegon, Optima, Groninger, Bausch+Ströbel, IMA) offering turnkey lines with their own isolators, which removes SKAN from bids it used to be specified into. In high-complexity niches (high-speed eBeam tub lines, aseptic-toxic ADC lines) intensity is lower and SKAN's position remains strong.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • An Optima regional sales manager (29-Jul-25; a competitor, so biased) puts SKAN's process-isolator share at about 50% from about 70% two years earlier and expects a gradual further decline over five years; he says there are bids where "nobody is offering third-party SKAN isolators". Management's own measure is a stable 30% to 40% global isolator share, and it says SKAN won most larger US fill-finish projects in H1 2026. The two measures are different (process isolators on lines versus all isolators), so both can be true: SKAN is losing share in standard turnkey line bids while holding the complex, high-value end.
  • Scale of rivals is rising: Syntegon reported record 2025 sales of EUR 1.75bn and a 16.1% EBITDA margin, integrated Telstar (freeze-drying plus isolation), launched the SynTiso gloveless high-speed isolator, and gained Apollo as a 37% investor in March 2026 to fund North American service expansion. Getinge launched ISOPRIME and ISOFLEX isolators and sells DPTE BetaBags, which compete with ABC Transfer.
  • Price-sensitive buyers (contract manufacturers, generic and biosimilar makers) are the fastest-growing customer groups and force comparable pricing across the main European suppliers; Chinese makers (Tofflon) and Esco (sterility-testing isolators in North America) attack standard segments.
  • Former management stated SKAN isolators cost up to 30%, sometimes 50%, more than competitors'. In a tender market that premium is exposed.

Interplay: The turnkey shift is a reinforcing loop against SKAN: each line a filler OEM delivers with its own isolator builds that OEM's isolator reference base and validation history, which makes the next turnkey bid more credible. SKAN's counter (partnerships with Groninger and Bausch+Ströbel, robotic integrated process systems) is real but depends on partners who are also competitors.

Modelling implication: Do not assume E&S price increases; assume flat-to-slightly-negative real price on standard equipment and keep E&S EBITDA margin structurally below the 2024 peak of 11.2% unless volume is well above 2024 levels.

C-05

How are input costs, input availability and supplier concentration changing, and is the net effect a headwind or a tailwind?

C-4515th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Input costs, availability and supplier concentration: headwind or tailwind?

Judgement: Slight headwind. Physical supply has normalised (component lead times back to pre-pandemic baselines, inventories being worked down), and steel is fabricated in-house, so availability is no longer a constraint. The cost pressure that matters is labour, not materials: SKAN is a Swiss engineering business whose personnel cost is about half of sales, and Swiss wage inflation plus specialist scarcity is the persistent input headwind. Fixed-price, non-indexed project contracts mean any cost inflation in the 12 to 36 months between order and delivery is absorbed by SKAN.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Personnel expenses: 45.5% of sales in 2024, 50.2% in 2025 (CHF 167.4m), 55.3% in H1 2026 (CHF 90.9m, +13.2%, including acquired staff). About half of roughly 1,700 to 1,755 employees sit in Allschwil.
  • Material and external services: 24.8% (2023), 26.4% (2024), 21.7% (H1 2025), 24.3% (H1 2026). In-sourcing in Görlitz and Stein reduced material intensity from 27.6% toward 25%.
  • Contracts: price changes require mutual written addenda based on the original cost basis, so older backlog cannot be repriced for inflation or a stronger franc. With a 12 to 36 month order-to-delivery cycle, this lag is long.
  • Supplier relationships exceed 25 years for key components, which secures quality but limits procurement leverage; new sanctions and country-of-origin documentation adds administrative overhead.

Modelling implication: Assume personnel cost per head rising 2% to 3% a year in Switzerland, no price indexation on equipment backlog, and material intensity of 23% to 26% of sales.

C-07

To what extent is a macro force acting on the company, on its sector and on its industry, and at which level does it bite hardest?

D4022nd of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Macro forces: company, sector or industry, and where they bite hardest

Judgement: The macro force that bites hardest is at company level, through the Swiss franc, because SKAN's cost base is heavily Swiss while over 80% of sales are earned abroad; next comes interest rates and biotech funding at industry level (customer capex, cell and gene therapy). Sector-level macro (defensive rotation into healthcare) has helped the share price but not the fundamentals.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Currency: translation cut reported growth by 3.7 points in 2023, 0.7 in 2024, 1.3 in 2025 and 1.8 in H1 2026 (CHF 3.8m of sales). Forward contracts are taken at contract inception and protect roughly twelve months; translation is unhedged. Management's sensitivity: a 10% fall in the euro against the franc has a net operational impact of about 3% to 4% (the source does not say whether this is percent of profit or margin points; my first-principles estimate, given half the personnel base in Switzerland and over 80% foreign revenue, is that a sustained broad 10% franc appreciation would cut EBITDA by roughly 15% to 25% once hedges roll off). The Change Forces report's separate estimate: a 5% move in USD, EUR and JPY changes EPS by about 3.6%. Morgan Stanley expects EUR/CHF at 0.89 by mid-2027; Deutsche Bank 0.915 by year-end; markets price two SNB hikes by September 2027 (ING). The skew is toward a stronger franc over 12 months.
  • Rates and funding: high rates and two years of biotech funding scarcity slowed cell and gene therapy and early-stage projects; Jefferies (3-Nov-26) sees biotech funding "warming up"; North American biotech equity issuance reached USD 37bn trailing twelve months. Jefferies notes funding takes about eighteen months to become commercial orders.
  • Market regime: the Fitness for Fighting digest reads risk-off beneath an index at its high; Healthcare and the Sector are Neutral; Switzerland Overweight; Fragility (small and complex) Double Underweight. SKAN is small, project-based and carries a governance overhang, which is exactly what Fragility penalises.

Modelling implication: Build an explicit FX line: model reported growth 1 to 2 points below constant currency each year at current forwards, and run a downside with EUR/CHF 0.85 and USD/CHF 0.75.

C-09

What other material external dynamic, positive or negative, is at work?

–46

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Other material external dynamics

Judgement: Four other dynamics matter, net slightly negative: (1) GLP-1 overbuild risk, (2) contract-manufacturer spare capacity, (3) the defensive rotation into European healthcare, which has lifted SKAN's multiple more than its fundamentals, and (4) the 2027 pharma capex plateau.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • GLP-1: 15% of 2024 orders; a CHF 40m GLP-1 project was suspended in design review and moved to 2027; Stevanato's single-customer GLP-1 moderation implies the leading GLP-1 maker is slowing capacity additions, the most likely source of further postponements or of the CHF 15m to 20m cancellation-risk item.
  • Spare capacity: the Sector Digest names "spare contract manufacturing capacity delaying equipment orders" and "long-term process capital spending digestion" as the most severe sector risk, suppressing new isolator orders at every fill-finish supplier at once.
  • Rotation: the European Healthcare memo of 18-Nov-26 judges the industry tailwind supportive but moderate over three months and fading to neutral over twelve unless 2027 guidance lifts estimates; more than half of the "money returning" source is used.
  • Capex plateau: USD 58.87bn in 2027, then -1.3% in 2028 (Deutsche Bank). SKAN's orders lead its revenue by 12 to 36 months, so a 2028 capex plateau would show in SKAN's orders in 2027 and revenue in 2028 to 2029.
C-35

To what extent are new entrants (from adjacent markets; standard entrants; orthogonal or innovative entrants) entering or threatening this market, and how much of a threat is each type?

F4431st of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading New entrants: adjacent, standard and orthogonal; how much of a threat is each?

Judgement: The adjacent entrants are the main threat and are already entering: filling-machine makers with in-house isolators (Syntegon, Optima, Bausch+Ströbel, IMA) and medtech groups expanding containment portfolios (Getinge). Standard entrants (lower-cost Chinese and other regional makers such as Tofflon, and niche players such as Esco) are a moderate threat confined so far to price-sensitive and standard segments; Western biopharma still prefers established premium suppliers for commercial lines. Orthogonal entrants (fully robotic, gloveless closed-system lines; cloud-orchestrated automation) are a long-term, low current threat, and SKAN is itself investing in robotics.

Reasoning: Adjacent entrants already own the customer relationship for the line and can bundle the isolator at low incremental cost; that is why they gained share fastest. Standard entrants face validation and reputation barriers that take years to cross in commercial GMP manufacturing.

C-39

Where relevant or material, how are suppliers behaving: a support to the company, or a source of risk?

C+5711th of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading Suppliers: support or source of risk?

Judgement: Suppliers are a support, with minor risks. SKAN fabricates stainless steel sheet and turned-milled parts in-house (Görlitz, Stein), holds buffer inventories of critical components, has relationships of more than 25 years with key suppliers, and component lead times have normalised. Residual risks: long lead times on some electrical components can shift revenue between periods; Asian-origin spare parts face higher US tariffs; inventory buffers carry obsolescence risk (the allowance for obsolete and slow-moving items rose from CHF 3.3m to 4.4m in 2025 while inventory fell).

Reasoning: Project lead times exceed component lead times, which absorbs most supply disruption. Raw steel is only 4% to 5% of system value.

Modelling implication: No supply constraint on growth; inventory roughly CHF 33m to 36m, trending with E&S activity.

1b

External tailwinds

7 answers
C-0164C-0350C-0472C-0866C-3258C-3772C-4364
C-01

To what extent is end-market demand changing, structurally or cyclically, and with how much visibility? (A cyclical swing counts when it has two-plus years of visibility.)

C+6413th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading End-market demand: structural or cyclical, and how visible?

Judgement: Demand for SKAN's isolators is structurally growing at roughly high single digits (the regulation-forced migration from open cleanrooms and restricted access barrier systems to sealed isolators, plus the biologics, antibody-drug conjugate (ADC) and biosimilar pipelines), overlaid by a pronounced capital-spending cycle that troughed in 2023 to 2025 and is now turning up. The last six months are clearly better than the prior twelve to eighteen: order intake +16.3% to 247.6 in H1 2026, book-to-bill 1.5, backlog +27.8% to a record 442.3, Americas orders +49.3%. Visibility is unusually good for 12 to 18 months (backlog at 1.22x trailing sales, projects extending into 2028) but the cyclical upswing does not yet have two-plus years of visibility, because customers now commit later, decide more selectively and can postpone delivery acceptance, as 2024 and 2025 proved.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Structural: EU GMP Annex 1 (effective August 2023) and FDA contamination-control expectations push new lines to isolators; an Optima expert says over 90% of new processing lines now integrate isolators and project values rose 15% to 20% a year on complexity; Annex 1 obliges upgrades across about 1,200 fill-finish suites (BEW Engineering, cited in the Sector Digest); aseptic process technology is put at about 8.5% CAGR; SKAN's order CAGR of 13.6% outgrew an 11% market historically.
  • Cyclical: post-pandemic vaccine digestion produced about 30 delayed projects at end-2024, a suspended CHF 40m GLP-1 project (pushed to 2027), and the 2025 sales fall of 7.7%. Customers lengthened decision cycles; firm orders across bioprocess equipment now come 3 to 6 months before delivery versus 12 months pre-pandemic (Deutsche Bank, 15-Oct-26).
  • The structural driver did not protect 2025: the backlog was large, yet sales fell. Backlog conversion, not order intake, is the binding constraint, because SKAN's revenue depends on customers' site readiness.
  • The peer cycle supports an upturn in equipment from late 2027: consumables have recovered (Sartorius, Getinge Life Science +16% organic in Q1 2026), equipment lags, and US onshoring orders are expected mainly in H2 2027 with revenue in 2028 (Sartorius via Bernstein). Lonza estimates only about 5% of about USD 600bn of announced US drug investment has broken ground: that is a long runway and a warning that announcements convert slowly. Deutsche Bank sees large-pharma capex peaking at USD 58.87bn in 2027 and falling 1.3% in 2028.

Skew and cone: Over twelve months the skew is mildly positive on orders (US greenfield slots converting, ADC and oncology lines, biosimilars) and negative on conversion timing (customer readiness). The cone is wide: 2025 showed that a few delayed projects can swing group sales by 10% and EBITDA by a third.

Modelling implication: Model demand as structural high-single to low-double-digit order growth with a cyclical overlay; drive E&S revenue from backlog conversion (roughly 55% to 65% of opening E&S backlog converting within twelve months is consistent with recent history), not from order intake directly, and keep a postponement haircut.

C-03

To what extent is a disruption force strengthening or being disproved, and in which direction does it act on the company?

D5026th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Disruption forces: strengthening or disproved, and in which direction?

Judgement: Three disruption forces act on SKAN, with opposite signs. (1) The cleanroom-to-isolator migration is a disruption SKAN benefits from and it is strengthening (regulation). (2) The rebundling of the fill-finish line by filling-machine makers (turnkey lines with in-house isolators) is a disruption acting against SKAN's best-of-breed model, and it is strengthening. (3) Robotic, gloveless and digitally integrated isolators are a technology shift that SKAN is participating in rather than losing to, so it is neutral to mildly positive for now. Longer-dated threats (oral GLP-1 peptides bypassing injectables, continuous processing) are not yet material in the 6-month horizon.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Migration: over 90% of new lines specify isolators; Annex 1 enforcement non-compliance findings reportedly rose 300% in 2024 versus 2023 (Morgan Stanley); about 50 SKAN eBeam systems installed, which management and the Earnings Review call the gold standard for high-speed tub lines.
  • Rebundling: in Wisdom Library terms this is "unbundle, capture, rebundle": SKAN captured the barrier layer when isolators were bought separately; filler OEMs are now rebundling the line under one purchase order and one project manager, which customers prefer to reduce site-integration risk (the first time a full line is assembled is at the customer site, and integration failures are where SKAN's most damaging profit threats sit, per the Risks report).
  • Technology: Syntegon's SynTiso gloveless line (up to 50% faster changeovers) and robot-based environmental monitoring patent raise the bar; SKAN's responses are robotic integrated process systems, the Sphere digital suite, Metronik manufacturing execution software, digital twins (first projects delivered H1 2026), VR mock-ups, an AI service tool, and nanox catalytic decontamination for cell therapy. These keep SKAN at parity or ahead in complex lines.
  • Oral peptides: flagged in the Business Foundations counter-case; Stevanato's 2027 low-single-digit outlook on lower GLP-1 volume from one customer (Jefferies, 18-Nov-26) shows that the GLP-1 injectable boom is already moderating. GLP-1 was about 15% of SKAN's 2024 orders.

Modelling implication: The net effect is a structural tailwind on market volume with a structural headwind on SKAN's share and price in standard lines. Model market growth of 8% to 10% but SKAN E&S share erosion of 1 to 3 points a year in standard process isolators, offset by mix toward higher-value complex lines.

C-04

To what extent is political or regulatory change under way, and how does it bear on the company?

A723rd of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Political or regulatory change: how does it bear on SKAN?

Judgement: Regulation is SKAN's single strongest external force and it is net positive and strengthening: Annex 1 enforcement and FDA contamination-control scrutiny create forced demand, and onshoring policy (US supply-chain security, BIOSECURE, the threat of 100% tariffs on imported generics from 2028) adds Western capacity build. Two regulatory frictions bite at the company level: Swissmedic certification delays for Pre-Approved Services (PAS), which have repeatedly pushed out SKAN's highest-margin initiative, and tariff administration. The CFO's criminal case is a legal, not a policy, matter and is treated under C-11 and C-86.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Annex 1: SKAN hosted nearly 700 regulators at Allschwil for inspector training and co-writes ISPE containment guidance, which embeds its designs as the reference standard.
  • Onshoring: J&J has committed more than USD 55bn to US facilities; Americas orders rebounded 49.3% to 98.7 in H1 2026, back to about 40% of group intake; the BIOSECURE list of companies of concern is due 18-Dec-26 and diverts demand to non-Chinese suppliers.
  • Tariffs: systems ship on Delivered-at-Place terms, so import duty is legally the customer's; US-bound isolators are built in Görlitz, Germany, so they bear the EU rate (capped at about 15%) rather than the Swiss rate; raw steel is only 4% to 5% of system value; spare-part price rises offset tariffs without client pushback. Exposure is manageable, though some clients ask SKAN to absorb duties and Asian-origin spare parts face higher rates.
  • PAS: the Swissmedic inspection slipped from H2 2025 to Q1 2026 (software validation), then to "within 2026"; non-GMP runs started in H1 2026; management now says meaningful revenue starts in 2027 and develops over three to four years. A further slip would extend a negative-cash investment phase.

Modelling implication: Hold a structural order tailwind from regulation through 2028 to 2030; separately, put PAS revenue on a delayed curve (first revenue 2027, not H2 2026) and make Swissmedic approval a binary switch for it.

C-08

To what extent is a key driver at the industry or sector level accelerating the company's demand?

C+6611th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is a key industry or sector driver accelerating SKAN's demand?

Judgement: Yes. Three drivers are accelerating at once: Annex 1-forced upgrades of legacy fill-finish suites, oncology and ADC aseptic-toxic lines (SKAN's highest-value niche, reported as "exceptional demand" with several double-digit-million orders), and US and European onshoring of sterile capacity. A fourth, biosimilars ahead of the biologics patent cliff, builds through 2027 to 2030. The Sector Digest's judgement is that SKAN "owns the equipment cycle now" and does not need the US mega-plants to be built, because Annex 1 upgrades and cancer-drug filling lines drive its orders.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Oncology and ADC: high-speed eBeam lines for ADCs drove E&S intake of 166.4 in H1 2026; toxic injectables cannot be terminally sterilised, so they require sealed isolators.
  • Onshoring: Americas intake +49.3%; several large US greenfield projects converted from slot reservations to orders; Sartorius expects onshoring equipment orders mainly in H2 2027.
  • Counter-drivers: GLP-1 moderating (Stevanato); cell and gene therapy only now recovering; vaccine capacity in surplus; contract-manufacturer spare capacity (Fujifilm deferred Denmark phase two beyond 2028; Lonza Vacaville).

Modelling implication: The mix is shifting toward larger, more complex, higher-value lines (project values +15% to 20% a year), which raises revenue per project and lengthens execution; model rising average order size and a longer conversion tail rather than more projects.

C-32

How is customer demand behaving: to what extent does it support the thesis, and how fragile is that support?

D5821st of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading Customer demand: how far does it support the thesis, and how fragile is that support?

Judgement: Customer demand supports the revenue thesis well for 2026 to 2027 (record backlog, book-to-bill 1.5, Americas orders +49%, customers paying CHF 132m of advances) but the support is fragile in two ways: concentration (a handful of large pharma and contract-manufacturer decisions move SKAN's year) and customer-controlled timing (projects slip when customers' sites, staff or validation are not ready, and SKAN cannot invoice the value-intensive phases).

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Concentration: in 2020 the top five clients were 43% of order intake, the next five 15%; the top 100 clients generated 70.6% of 2012 to 2023 intake, recurring clients 71%; end markets 81% biopharma, 14% contract manufacturers, 4% cell and gene (2018 to 2020).
  • Timing: about 30 projects slipped at end-2024 because customers were not ready to accept delivery; a CHF 40m GLP-1 project was suspended in design review; vaccine lines were deferred; about CHF 40m of backlog remains delayed and CHF 15m to 20m is at risk of cancellation.
  • Behaviour: customers decide more selectively and want integrated lines; drug-pricing pressure in the US raises scrutiny of capital spending but has not stopped capacity investment; contract-manufacturer capital budgets moved to slight growth (Jefferies, 10-Sep-26).
  • Advances are a strong signal of commitment but also a reversal risk: if orders slow, advances unwind and operating cash flow falls (2023: operating cash flow fell to CHF 8.7m from 61.5m).

Modelling implication: Use a postponement factor on E&S conversion (for example 5% to 10% of planned annual E&S revenue slipping a year in a normal year, 15% to 20% in a bad year like 2025), and link customer advances to order intake.

C-37

What are regulators and political actors doing, does it help or hurt the company, and how fragile is that position?

A723rd of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading Regulators and political actors: what they are doing, the effect, and how fragile

Judgement: Regulators are SKAN's strongest ally and the support is durable: Annex 1 and FDA contamination-control expectations push the industry to isolators and SKAN is embedded in how regulators are trained. Political actors are net helpful (US and European onshoring, BIOSECURE, generic tariffs that would force US capacity), with manageable friction from tariffs. The fragile point is company-specific: Swissmedic's approval of PAS, which has slipped and is a binary switch for SKAN's highest-margin growth line.

Reasoning: Regulatory standards rarely loosen once tightened; the direction is persistent. US drug-pricing policy (most-favoured-nation pricing pressure) could make pharma capex more selective but has not stopped it. Tariff escalation beyond about 30% on EU-made equipment would hurt competitiveness against any future US local maker, of which there is none at the high end today.

C-43

How large is the realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and what rate of growth does it allow for over the mid term?

C6414th of 41

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

The Investment Analyst's heading Realistic addressable market and the growth it allows

Judgement: The realistic core market (isolators and their aftermarket for aseptic and aseptic-toxic fill-finish, laboratory and sterility-testing isolators, and cell and gene therapy isolators) is roughly CHF 0.8bn to 1.1bn today (my inference from SKAN's 30% to 40% share and its sales), growing at about 8% to 10% a year (aseptic process technology 8.5% CAGR; addressable markets in the sector 7% to 9%; SKAN historically outgrew an 11% market with 13.6% order growth). Adjacencies (validated consumables, software, PAS, integrated robotic process systems) widen the addressable market by several hundred million francs. This allows SKAN mid-teens growth through about 2028 (market growth plus rising project values plus S&C attach plus PAS), but tripling sales to about CHF 1bn, as the 2025 Capital Markets Day aspired to, would require either holding share in a market that filler OEMs are entering, or making adjacencies (consumables, software, PAS) several times larger than today.

Reasoning: Annex 1 obliges upgrades of about 1,200 fill-finish suites; over 90% of new lines specify isolators; ADC and biosimilar capacity is being added; US onshoring adds greenfield sites. The addressable market is not the constraint for 2026 to 2028; SKAN's share and its execution capacity are.

Modelling implication: Model E&S at market growth minus 1 to 2 points of share drift plus a few points of rising project value (net 8% to 12% a year), and S&C at 12% to 18% organic plus PAS; together these support group growth of 12% to 16% in 2027 to 2029, close to consensus.

1c

Overall external picture

2 answers
C-0668C-1058
C-06

Where is the company's own revenue and demand cycle, and to what extent is it turning?

B688th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading The company's own revenue and demand cycle: where is it and is it turning?

Judgement: SKAN's own cycle troughed in H1 2025 and turned up decisively in H1 2026; the turn is confirmed on orders, backlog and sales, but not yet on margin quality or cash. The company is in the early-to-middle phase of a recovery that the backlog should carry through 2027.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Half-year sales growth: H1-24 +17.2%, H2-24 +9.6%, H1-25 -17.8%, H2-25 +0.6%, H1-26 +22.2% (+24.0% at constant currency).
  • Order intake: 2022 411.7 (pandemic peak), 2023 295.1 (-28%), 2024 359.5 (+21.8%), 2025 about 383 (book-to-bill 1.15), H1 2026 247.6 (+16.3%); trailing-twelve-month intake about 418 against trailing sales about 363.
  • E&S EBITDA swung from -9.1 (H1-25) to +2.3 (H1-26), but H1 margins remain far below the H2 run-rate of 14% to 15%, and S&C margin fell to 20.1%.
  • Quality of the turn: the book-to-bill of 1.5 includes conversion of engineering contracts and slot reservations into full system orders, which flatters H1 intake; Asia orders fell to 9.1 from 20.8; Americas sales fell 6.2% even as Americas orders surged, so US revenue recovery is an H2 2026 and 2027 event.

Modelling implication: 2026 sales growth of 16% to 19% is supported by backlog; 2027 growth of 12% to 16% is supported if H2 2026 intake stays at or above 200. The first point at which the cycle could turn down again on the current evidence is 2028 (pharma capex plateau), not 2027.

C-10

Taking each sector and industry thematic driver in turn, and then all of the above together: how strong are the external headwinds and how strong are the tailwinds at value-chain, industry and sector level, and what is the overall external change picture?

C+5812th of 41

Layer 1 · External change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading External headwinds and tailwinds by thematic driver and overall

Judgement: The external picture is a strong structural tailwind on volume (regulation, oncology and ADC, onshoring, biosimilars) partly cancelled by three structural headwinds that act on SKAN's share, price and margin (turnkey rebundling by filler OEMs, price-conscious buyers and Chinese entrants, and the Swiss franc cost base), with a cyclical upswing that is real but has 12 to 18 months of visibility rather than two to three years. Net: moderately positive for revenue, neutral-to-negative for margin.

By driver:

  • Sector driver 1, "drug makers' consumables then equipment" (Sector Digest): tailwind, strengthening; for SKAN the equipment leg is earlier than for peers because Annex 1 and ADC lines are not waiting for the US mega-plants. Value-chain level.
  • Sector driver 2, cost programmes: not applicable as a sector tailwind for SKAN; SKAN is investing, not cutting.
  • Sector driver 3, onshoring (industry level: helps drugmakers first, plant owners next, equipment makers later): tailwind for SKAN from H2 2026 orders to 2028 revenue.
  • Value-chain power shift: pricing power in fill-finish equipment is moving from the machine to the aftermarket (Researcher, 1-Nov-26): a headwind for E&S, a tailwind for S&C, which is why the S&C margin trend is so important (see C-53).
  • Macro: franc, rates and the 2027 to 2028 capex plateau are headwinds.

Overall: Tailwinds outweigh headwinds on revenue through 2027; margin tailwinds depend on SKAN's own levers (PAS, S&C, utilisation), not on the environment.

Internal change forces/risks

–not yet rated

Memo topic 2, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

Internal change forces

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group. The rating above stands for it, so it carries no number of its own.

SKAN is moving from a founder-era build-out (hire ahead, invest ahead, acquire into services) to an execution phase under an external operator from a high-discipline culture. That transition is the core of the internal change case: capacity, people and acquisitions are in place, capex is set to fall by about half as a share of sales, and the first half of 2026 showed cost discipline. It is offset by a genuine governance overhang (a convicted CFO retained on appeal), an incomplete leadership bench in the segment that is meant to drive margins, and an M&A step that has so far diluted rather than lifted the S&C margin. For the model, the internal forces argue for: opex growth below sales growth, falling capex intensity, no capital returns beyond the dividend, explicit minorities, and a margin path that assumes no help from S&C mix until the H2 2026 print proves the S&C margin has stabilised.

Internal risks

Group judgement

Risks

F3437th of 41

Layer 1 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The risk profile is dominated by a small number of correlated, customer-controlled events (postponement or cancellation of large projects) acting on a cost base with 65% to 70% decremental margins, overlaid by a governance overhang and the sentiment fragility of a small, illiquid, thinly covered stock that has rallied 48% on multiple expansion. The core engine is re-accelerating, so the base case is not deterioration; but the cone of outcomes is wide and skewed down: the fair trough in a plausible bear case is about 36% to 43% below today, against consensus-based upside that the share price has largely already discounted. For the third set, the predictability assessment should centre on H2 2026 conversion, the CHF 15m to 20m cancellation item, the PAS approval and the CFO appeal.

C-14

To what extent are standards improving in execution, operational measures, governance or cost discipline? (choices and actions)

F5437th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Are standards improving in execution, operations, governance or cost discipline?

Judgement: Partly, and early. There is first evidence of improved cost discipline and execution in H1 2026 (operating expenses up 13.8% against constant-currency sales up 24.0%; other operating expenses down 9.5%; E&S back to positive EBITDA; fewer project delays), and a governance improvement in pay design (share-based short- and long-term incentives from 2026). Against this, three standards have not yet improved: S&C margin (falling), cash conversion (operating cash flow down 27.7% in H1 2026 while EBITDA rose), and the reliability of the PAS timeline (repeated slips caused partly by SKAN's own software validation).

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Execution: management pre-produced standard isolators during 2025 gaps to protect utilisation; standardisation (VARIOSYS, modular components) reduced engineering hours; orders now split roughly 50/50 between large customised systems and smaller standard isolators, which smooths factory loading.
  • People: voluntary plus involuntary turnover fell from 10.4% (2024) to 8.6% (2025); involuntary turnover stable at 4.7%.
  • Governance: no bonus was paid for 2025 (55% goal achievement), showing the pay system bites; a share-based plan replaces the previous cash-only system. But the audit committee includes a 10.6% shareholder (Gregor Plattner), the board retained a convicted CFO, and the board has never had a long-term incentive plan until 2026.
  • Weak standards: the Leadership report rates planning standards "normal rather than exceptional"; the 2025 miss came after management had raised margin guidance; the PAS inspection slipped because "complex software validation requires more effort than scheduled".

Modelling implication: Allow for some opex discipline (operating expense growth below sales growth by 3 to 6 points in growth years), but do not assume the S&C margin recovers to 2024 levels without evidence from the H2 2026 print.

C-86

What negative corporate change developments are present/plausible?

F3039th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement: The negative developments are concentrated in governance, key people and organisational strain. (1) The CFO's first-instance conviction and the board's decision to retain him, which extends a governance overhang for the duration of the appeal and risks a forced CFO change during the new CEO's first year. (2) Thin senior bench in services: the Chief Service Officer resigned and the role was doubled up, just as services became the strategic priority. (3) Organisational strain from scaling: the workforce roughly doubled in five years (about 1,755 staff), personnel costs are about half of sales, and SKAN itself discloses the risk of quality deficiencies and errors from rapid growth and inexperienced staff. (4) The PAS timeline has slipped repeatedly. (5) A backlog item of CHF 15m to 20m at risk of cancellation (a single customer, carried since end-2025) and about CHF 40m of delayed backlog.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Board composition: six members, five called independent, but the audit committee includes Gregor Plattner (10.6% holder) and the former CEO sits on the board; Axiom Investors voted against several directors in 2023 on independence grounds. One woman among six directors.
  • Alignment: insiders own about 12.5%, almost all the Plattner family; the CEO started with zero shares; the CFO holds 0.1%; no insider dealing in twelve months.
  • Plausible future negatives: a failed appeal and abrupt CFO exit; cancellation of the CHF 15m to 20m item; a further S&C margin decline; a repeat of H2 delivery slippage like 2024.

Modelling implication: None directly, but keep a probability-weighted downside scenario with one delivery miss in the next four half-years, as history suggests (see C-69).

C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

C-5418th of 41

Layer 1 · Company - Management

The Investment Analyst's heading How holistic and congruent is the culture?

Judgement: Moderately congruent, not at the level of a Lifco or Avanza. Positive signs: ten explicit values, monthly CEO calls, an academy implementing over 90% of training requests, Great Place to Work certification, falling turnover (10.4% to 8.6%), a low pay ratio (8 to 1), no bonuses paid in a bad year, and decentralisation toward subsidiaries. Incongruent signals: executives had no equity incentives until 2026 (pay and owner thinking were not linked); the board retained a convicted CFO, which sits uneasily with the compliance messaging; rapid growth (the workforce roughly doubled in five years) strains integration, and SKAN itself flags quality risks from inexperienced staff; strategy says "recurring margin" while the S&C margin falls.

C-81

What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

F3536th of 41

Layer 1 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading Sudden, high-magnitude, low-probability fundamental risks

Judgement: Six such risks matter, ranked by expected damage. The first two are the most plausible; the rest are tail risks.

  1. A large project cancelled or postponed again (plausibility medium; magnitude CHF 15m to 40m of sales, CHF 8m to 25m of EBITDA in the year it hits). A CHF 15m to 20m item is already at risk of cancellation with one customer; about CHF 40m is delayed (including a CHF 40m GLP-1 project moved to 2027); Stevanato's single-customer GLP-1 reset raises the chance that a GLP-1 maker reconsiders capacity. With 65% to 70% decremental margins, one such event is a 15% to 40% EBITDA hit.
  2. A failed CFO appeal followed by an abrupt exit, or new legal findings (plausibility medium over 12 to 36 months; magnitude moderate operationally, larger for the multiple). It would come during the new chief executive's first years and could disrupt investor relations and capital planning.
  3. Swissmedic refuses or long delays PAS approval (plausibility low to medium; magnitude: the about CHF 70m asset under-earns, the up-to CHF 20m to 25m EBITDA option recedes, and a write-down is possible).
  4. A quality or contamination failure, or a site-integration failure on a complex line, attributed to SKAN (plausibility low; magnitude potentially large through warranty, delay penalties and reputation). SKAN itself discloses quality risk from rapid scaling and inexperienced staff; isolators are becoming more complex.
  5. A filler partner switches to its own isolators across its bids (plausibility low to medium; magnitude: a meaningful slice of E&S intake within one to two years).
  6. A currency shock (franc appreciation of 10% or more against the euro and dollar; plausibility medium given forecasts of EUR/CHF 0.89; magnitude by my estimate 15% to 25% of EBITDA once hedges roll off).

Correlation: 1, 4 and 6 tend to cluster in a capex downturn; 2 is independent. The Risks report rates the joint probability of project and cash failures as high during customer design reviews.

D-03

In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

F369th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading How diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences between the forecasts and the last four to six years?

Judgement: Concentrated, and for the next 18 months close to a single driver. The level of profit is increasingly diffuse; the change in profit is not. S&C, which rests on more than 1,100 installed isolators and hundreds of customers, supplies about 57% of forecast EBITDA in 2026 and 2027 (about 32 of 56, and about 39 of 70), which is a real floor under the level. But two-thirds of the increase in EBITDA to 2027, and nearly all of its variance, comes from one thing: the timing at which a few dozen E&S projects reach their manufacturing and assembly phases, where 75% to 80% of a project's value is recognised and which customers control. Over four years two further drivers join it (PAS, and the 2027 order intake that sets 2028 and 2029 revenue), and one driver is fully in the company's hands (capital spending falling from 13% of sales to about 8%). The forecasts differ from history through few drivers, and the most important is the least controllable.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • How the forecasts differ from 2020 to 2025. Slower revenue growth (11.6% a year from the depressed 2025 base, about 8% from the 2024 peak, against 17% in 2020 to 2024); a higher margin (14.5% rising to about 17%, against 13.7% to 15.8%); positive free cash flow (about 160 cumulative in 2026 to 2030 on my inputs, against about -40 in 2021 to 2025); S&C at 38% to 44% of sales against 25% to 35%.
  • What generates each difference. Margin to 2027: E&S volume absorption (about 21 of the 31 EBITDA increase) and S&C growth (about 10). Margin from 2027 to 2030: PAS (about 12 to 13 of the 30 increase), S&C compounding and slower growth of fixed costs. Free cash flow: capital spending (about 46 in 2025 to 30 to 38 a year, worth about 20 a year against the old intensity and the one driver that needs nothing from customers), EBITDA, and customer advances, which are the same driver as E&S orders seen earlier. Revenue growth: E&S conversion to 2027, then the order cycle.
  • The drivers are fewer than they look, because several are one driver. Customer advances move with E&S orders. S&C timing is not independent of E&S: maintenance and retrofits were postponed alongside projects in H1 2025, when S&C sales were flat. The franc acts on all of them slowly. Only PAS (Swissmedic and customer uptake) is truly independent, and it matters only from 2028.
  • How concentrated the main driver is. In 2025 one suspended GLP-1 project of about CHF 40m and a handful of vaccine lines took group sales from guided mid-teens growth to -7.7% and EBITDA down by a third. In 2020 the top five customers were 43% of order intake. The H1 2026 order book was built on several orders "in the double-digit million range" for oncology lines and United States projects. A project of CHF 30m to 40m whose manufacturing phase moves out of a year takes perhaps CHF 15m to 30m of sales with it and, at a 65% to 70% drop-through, CHF 10m to 20m of EBITDA, or 15% to 30% of 2027's.
  • The floor. If E&S in 2027 only repeated 2025 (EBITDA of 9.4) while S&C delivered, group EBITDA would be about 49 and attributable EPS about 0.96, which is the bear case. The diffuse part of the business protects the company and the dividend; it does not protect the forecast.
  • Peers. The AlphaSense report on dispersion reaches the same verdict (drivers "concentrated rather than diffuse") and adds the comparison that matters: the more stable project companies are those with the larger service share (GEA 40%, Getinge's recurring share about 67%), not those with the larger backlog. SKAN's mix is moving that way but in 2027 the swing segment is still 61% of sales.

Internal corporate change forces

C-11

To what extent have the chief executive, or other key leaders (e.g. finance director, operations director, board, division heads, etc.) or other key actors (e.g. activists, major shareholders) changed, and what does the change signal?

F4039th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Changes in the chief executive, key leaders and key actors, and what they signal

Judgement: Two changes dominate and they point in opposite directions. The positive one: an external operator, Jonas Greutert, replaced the founding-era chief executive Thomas Huber on 1 January 2026, which signals a deliberate shift from building capacity for growth to extracting returns from it (operational excellence, segment-level profit accountability). The negative one: the chief financial officer, Burim Maraj, was convicted of insider trading at first instance on 17 November 2026, has appealed, and the board has kept him in office. The CFO is the author of the May 2025 Capital Markets Day targets and the company's long-standing financial memory; his position is now uncertain for 12 to 36 months, in the first year of a new chief executive.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Greutert: ETH Zürich doctorate in computer science and Rochester MBA; Dätwyler IT Infra (2008 to 2015); Mettler-Toledo from 2015, head of the Product Inspection Division and a member of the Group Executive Board for five years (2020 to 2025), a division of about 15% of Mettler's sales serving pharmaceutical quality control. Mettler-Toledo is a benchmark for pricing discipline and productivity programmes; my conjecture, not yet evidenced, is that Greutert will import that discipline (segment accountability was the first organisational change he announced). The caveat in the Leadership report: Mettler's matrix concentrated final financial authority at group level, so his independent capital-allocation record is unproven, and his own SKAN record is one half-year.
  • Huber moved to the board (non-independent); he oversaw the IPO and eight years of expansion. His presence secures continuity and client relationships but limits board independence and may slow change.
  • The CFO case: the Office of the Attorney General opened the investigation in May 2023; the former chairman's case was dismissed with costs borne by the state; the head of the services segment was acquitted; the CFO was convicted and appealed. The board's choice to retain him rests on the presumption of innocence; the Leadership report notes a conviction would normally trigger an exit. Directors and officers registered no share dealings in twelve months, so there are no insider signals.
  • Other changes: Chief Service Officer Maria Cuevas Otero resigned in October 2025 and Martin Steegmüller absorbed the role alongside his existing role (Chief Pure Solutions Officer, digital), during the period when services are the strategic priority; Chief Innovation Officer Sascha Pawel became an adviser in 2024; Chairman Gert Thoenen retired in 2024 (Beat Lüthi succeeded); Patrick Schär left the board in May 2026 after 16 years; Dr Christian Schlögel (software and digital transformation) joined the board in May 2026.

Signal and skew: The chief executive change signals that the board wants margin and execution, not more expansion. The CFO outcome is the main governance tail risk: if the appeal fails, SKAN must recruit a CFO during a reorganisation; if it succeeds, the overhang lifts. A forced CFO change could, paradoxically, become a positive (the Wisdom Library's "CFO refresh as a leading indicator of a capital-discipline reset"), but the market will treat the interim as an overhang.

Modelling implication: No direct line-item effect; treat as a multiple and execution-risk factor. Expect management bandwidth to be partly absorbed by legal proceedings through 2027.

C-12

To what extent have the strategy or the strategic priorities changed, and how well is the change articulated and resourced? (choices)

D6320th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Strategy and strategic priorities: how much change, how well articulated and resourced?

Judgement: The strategy itself (Vision 2030: fortify market leadership, expand the addressable market into integrated process systems, grow the aftermarket toward a 50/50 sales split, and digitalise) has not changed since the May 2025 Capital Markets Day; what changed in 2026 is the emphasis, from building capacity to executing on it. The strategy is well articulated and was well resourced, arguably over-resourced ahead of demand: the capacity is largely built, and the coming years test whether it earns a return.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Articulation: four pillars, explicit mid-term targets (mid-to-upper teens sales growth, EBITDA margin gradually to the upper teens), a stated 50/50 E&S to S&C ambition, a PAS revenue target (about CHF 50m at up to 50% EBITDA margin at full capacity), and an aspiration to triple sales and quadruple EBITDA in the mid-term (CFO at the May 2025 CMD; a time frame is not given).
  • Resourcing: capex 35.3 (2023), 53.8 (2024), 45.9 (2025), mostly the PAS facility (total about CHF 70m), Görlitz steelwork and assembly, Stein and Allschwil offices; growth capex of CHF 100m to 120m over the mid term was guided at the CMD; R&D and strategic initiatives 7% to 8% of sales, fully expensed; about 284 staff added in 2025 (208 by acquisition) after 216 in 2023; two acquisitions in 2025 funded by bank debt.
  • 2026 shifts under Greutert: a new structure with clear segment-level financial accountability; "operational excellence" and a gradual rise in margin to the upper teens; a "highly selective project start approach" for cell and gene therapy; first digital twin projects; next-generation eBeam; winning major US fill-finish lines.
  • Weak points: the 50/50 target is being reached partly by acquiring lower-margin, project-like businesses (Metronik is a systems integrator), which reintroduces lumpiness into the "recurring" segment; PAS moves SKAN into regulated manufacturing services, a different business with its own validation risk and a possible channel conflict with contract manufacturers who are also customers.

Modelling implication: The investment phase is ending: consensus capex falls from 13% of sales in 2025 to 7% to 8% in 2026 to 2028. If that holds, free cash flow improves mechanically even without margin gains. The strategic payoff to model is (1) PAS from 2027, (2) E&S utilisation of capacity already built, and (3) S&C growth, but with a realistic S&C margin (see C-53).

C-13

To what extent has financial guidance been raised, or have mid-term targets or capital-markets-day ambitions been set or raised? (choices)

D4827th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Has guidance been raised, or have mid-term targets been set or raised?

Judgement: No guidance has been raised in the period. 2026 guidance (high-teens sales growth, 13% to 15% EBITDA margin) was set in March 2026 and confirmed in August 2026. The ambitious mid-term targets were set in May 2025, months before the company's largest miss, and have been reiterated rather than raised. The pattern of the last eighteen months is a rebasing, not a raising: 2025 guidance (mid-teens growth, 14% to 16% margin, itself raised by one margin point from 2024's range) was abandoned in a profit warning on 6 November 2025.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • 2026 guidance sits at the level of consensus (FactSet: sales 396, +18.7%; EBITDA margin 14.1%); it has not been raised despite the strong H1 orders, which is consistent with a management that knows its revenue depends on customers' readiness in Q4.
  • The mid-term ambition (triple sales, quadruple EBITDA) was presented by the CFO now under conviction; a new chief executive could recalibrate it without personal embarrassment. The first formal opportunity is the FY 2026 results on 23 March 2027 (2027 guidance) or a new Capital Markets Day.
  • Consensus already embeds the mid-term targets: sales growth of 15% to 16% a year in 2027 and 2028 and a margin of 17.3% by 2029.

Modelling implication: There is no guidance momentum to extrapolate. The asymmetry for the March 2027 guidance is to the downside on margin (new CEO's first full-year guide; a natural moment to set a beatable bar) and neutral on sales (backlog supports mid-teens).

C-14To what extent are standards improving in execution, operational measures, governance or cost discipline?F5437th of 41full answer under 2a Internal risks
C-15

To what extent is the company reducing its perimeter or composition, through divestments, simplification or exits from low-return units? (actions)

F4536th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is the company reducing its perimeter or composition?

Judgement: No. SKAN is expanding its perimeter, not shrinking it: two acquisitions in 2025 (Metronik 76%, ABC Transfer 84%), Aseptic Technologies raised to 90%, Plast4Life injection moulding integrated, a new PAS subsidiary (Maeven AG, February 2025), a planned US production facility, and decentralised growth in subsidiaries (about 60% of workforce growth). There have been no divestments or exits. This is neither good nor bad in itself, but it reduces predictability: the group now has more businesses, more minorities and more integration work than a year ago.

Reasoning: Simplification is not a relevant lever at SKAN today because there are no obvious low-return units to exit (the low-return unit in 2025 was the core E&S business, temporarily). The relevant question is whether added complexity is managed: the new segment accountability structure is the answer management is offering.

Modelling implication: Model three to four minority interests (Metronik 24%, ABC Transfer 16%, Aseptic Technologies 10%): in H1 2026 net profit was 5.4 and profit attributable to shareholders 4.8, a leakage of about 11%; also consider future purchase obligations for the remaining stakes (not disclosed in the files).

C-16

To what extent is the company entering new geographic or product markets, and how sensibly? (actions)

C-6016th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is the company entering new geographic or product markets, and how sensibly?

Judgement: Yes, on four fronts, and mostly sensibly: (1) North America, where SKAN is the leading high-end isolator supplier without a local high-end competitor and is now adding service capacity and a small assembly facility; (2) consumables (ABC Transfer bags and ports, Aseptic Technologies closed vials), which create validated, recurring pull-through; (3) software (Metronik manufacturing execution systems), which creates switching costs; and (4) Pre-Approved Services, a pay-per-use filling service, which is the most novel, highest-margin and highest-risk move. The first three are adjacent extensions of the installed base; the fourth is a new business model.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • US: Americas sales were 28% of 2025 sales and orders rebounded to about 40% of H1 2026 intake; the US had the highest staff turnover (15.2% in 2024); the Risks report notes US customers are more cautious, US growth is service-led, and know-how transfer for local assembly takes years. A US factory was planned for 2027 or 2028. Görlitz already ships US isolators on EU tariff terms.
  • Consumables: ABC Transfer competes head-on with Getinge's DPTE BetaBags (Getinge's sterile transfer business grew double digits); Aseptic Technologies' closed vials are used in eight approved drugs with several hundred molecules in development.
  • PAS: SKAN invested about CHF 70m in a facility near Allschwil; target about CHF 50m of revenue at up to 50% EBITDA margin; ramp three years from commercial start; meaningful revenue only from 2027. The logic is sound (customers shorten time to market by running stability batches on a pre-qualified line while their own line is built), but it puts SKAN into GMP manufacturing, where Swissmedic, quality systems and capacity utilisation determine returns, and it depends on customer adoption that has never been proven at scale.

Modelling implication: Treat PAS as a separate revenue line with an explicit ramp (for example 10% to 20% utilisation in 2027 rising over three years), its own fixed cost base (already being incurred) and depreciation on about CHF 70m of assets.

C-17

To what extent is the company raising prices in real terms (above cost inflation), and are the rises real, merely nominal, or so far only latent or plausible and increasingly noted by management? (actions)

D4624th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is the company raising prices in real terms?

Judgement: Real price increases are evident only in services, spare parts and consumables, where customers accept them because isolator maintenance and requalification are mission-critical. In equipment, which is about two-thirds of sales, there is no evidence of real price increases: the market moved to competitive tenders, fixed-price contracts are not indexed, and SKAN's historical 30% to 50% premium is under pressure. Rising average project values (+15% to 20% a year) reflect complexity and scope, not like-for-like pricing. Pricing power in equipment is at best latent, and could become real if the new chief executive brings Mettler-Toledo-style value pricing, which management has not yet mentioned.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Services: "SKAN implemented price increases for critical spare parts and consumables over the last twelve months... blue-chip clients accepted these higher service prices without significant negative feedback"; spare-part increases offset tariffs; a 1 to 1.5 year US spare-parts buffer was built.
  • Equipment: quotation success rate about 50%; "must fight much more to win"; price-conscious contract manufacturers and biosimilar makers force comparable pricing; partners who passed full tariffs were "immediately blacklisted", so tactful consolidation is needed.
  • The value proposition for premium pricing remains (validation risk, complexity, process warranty, eBeam), which is why management can hold premium in complex niches.

Modelling implication: Price in S&C: +2% to +4% a year. Price in E&S: zero real, with tender discounts possible in standard lines. Older backlog carries cost inflation without price relief.

C-18

To what extent is the company stepping up mergers and acquisitions, and how strategically sensible, disciplined and well integrated are they? (actions)

D5022nd of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is the company stepping up M&A, and how sensible, disciplined and well integrated?

Judgement: M&A stepped up in 2025 (two deals in one month after years of organic growth) and is strategically coherent, buying consumables lock-in (ABC Transfer) and a digital backbone (Metronik) to shift the mix toward recurring revenue. Discipline is uncertain because prices were not disclosed (my estimate from the cash-flow statement is about CHF 85m to 90m combined, roughly three times annualised acquired sales of about CHF 28m to 30m), and integration is in its first year. The early financial evidence is mixed: the acquisitions added CHF 14.2m of H1 2026 sales and lifted S&C to 39% of sales, but the S&C margin fell, which means either the acquired businesses are lower-margin than the legacy service base or integration and service build-out costs are absorbing their contribution.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • S&C incremental margins: 2025 sales +26.1, EBITDA +2.5 (about 10% incremental); H1 2026 sales +20.7, EBITDA +3.0 (about 15% incremental), against a legacy segment margin of 28% to 30%.
  • A sell-side analyst (Hans Alig) calculated that excluding Metronik the organic S&C margin fell to the "lower teens". The arithmetic does not obviously reconcile with the reported 20.1% unless the acquisitions carry a very high margin; whichever way it is split, the incremental margin of S&C growth over the last eighteen months is far below the segment average. Management attributes the H1 dip to timing (personnel costs recognised immediately, service activities deferred to H2).
  • Funding: bank debt (total debt from 10.6 to 131); the reported equity ratio fell to 26.1% because goodwill is offset against equity under Swiss GAAP FER (adjusted above 40%).
  • Management states acquisitions must be immediately profitable and support S&C it has not mooted further deals; the leverage headroom is modest (0.8x).

Modelling implication: Model acquired sales of about CHF 28m to 30m a year growing high single digits, at a margin nearer 15% to 20% than 30%, and keep minorities.

C-19

To what extent is the company making more use of its balance sheet (for investment, acquisitions or returns), or stepping up shareholder returns (a buyback starting or increasing, a special or progressive dividend), or is management increasingly mooting either? (actions)

F4033rd of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Is the company making more use of the balance sheet or stepping up shareholder returns?

Judgement: The balance sheet was used once, in 2025, for debt-funded acquisitions, moving SKAN from net cash of about CHF 51m to net debt of about CHF 42m (0.8x EBITDA). Shareholder returns went the other way: the dividend was cut to CHF 0.22 from 0.40 (the 30% payout policy was kept), there is no buyback (6,850 shares were bought only to cover incentive plans), and management is not mooting either. Unused credit lines fell from CHF 66m to CHF 32m. This is not a capital-return story for the next 18 months.

Reasoning: Free cash flow has been negative or marginal for five years (cumulative about -40m on FactSet's definition) and is expected to stay small (consensus FCF 8, 18, 22 for 2026 to 2028). Customer advances of CHF 132m fund the working capital but are a liability that reverses if orders fall, so management rightly keeps liquidity. Capacity for further balance-sheet use exists only once PAS goes live and capex falls.

Modelling implication: Dividend at about 30% of EPS; no buybacks; net debt broadly flat to falling slowly from about 42m; interest expense about 2m to 3m on fixed-rate bank debt of about 131m.

C-20

What other material positive corporate change forces are present/plausible?

–60

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Other material positive corporate change forces

Judgement: Five other positive forces are present or plausible. (1) The end of the heavy investment phase: capex falling from 13% to about 7% to 8% of sales while capacity is in place to carry growth to roughly CHF 500m of sales. (2) Operational standardisation and pre-production, which reduce engineering hours and smooth utilisation. (3) A new chief executive from a high-discipline operating culture, with segment accountability already introduced. (4) Share-based incentives from 2026, which for the first time tie executive pay to multi-year share price performance. (5) Technology leadership in complex lines (eBeam, aseptic-toxic, robotics, digital twins), which protects the high-value end of the market where SKAN's premium is least contested.

Reasoning: The first is the most important for the model, because it converts the same EBITDA into much more free cash flow: with EBITDA of about 70 in 2027 and capex of about 33, free cash flow can rise toward 25 to 30 if advances hold. The third is plausible but unproven; it would show as a narrowing gap between sales growth and personnel cost growth and as better H1 margins (less extreme H1/H2 skew).

C-87

To what extent have key controlling actors (e.g. activists, major shareholders) indicated scope for change in strategy/approach?

F4536th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement: No. The controlling shareholders (the Plattner family with about 21.2% across Gregor and Vera Plattner, and Dr Willy Michel with 10.5% to 14.7%, the files disagree) have made no public statements about strategy, there are no activists, and voting rules (two-thirds supermajority for key changes, registration limits) entrench the status quo. The only revealed preference of the controlling group is the board's decision to hire an external operator as chief executive, which signals a wish for execution, and its decision to keep the CFO, which signals loyalty and stability over governance optics.

Reasoning: With a third of the shares in two family groups, no activist can force change, and a bid is implausible without their consent. That stabilises strategy but removes an external catalyst; it also means minority shareholders rely on the board's own judgement, which was questioned by a proxy vote in 2023.

C-21

Taken together, how strong, broad and well-resourced are the positive corporate changes under way?

F5234th of 41

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

The Investment Analyst's heading Taken together: how strong, broad and well resourced are the positive corporate changes?

Judgement: Moderate in strength, narrow in breadth, and well resourced in money but thinly resourced in leadership bandwidth. The single most important positive change is the transition from an investment phase to an execution phase under a new operator; it is resourced by capacity already built and by a balance sheet with modest leverage. It is narrow because most of the improvement in 2026 to 2027 rests on three things (E&S backlog conversion, PAS approval and ramp, S&C margin recovery), each of which has slipped before. Leadership bandwidth is the binding constraint: a new CEO, a CFO under conviction and on appeal, and a vacant-then-doubled-up services leadership.

Reasoning: Change forces that would have made this case strong (a raised guide, a new CFO with a discipline mandate, demonstrated S&C margin recovery, a dated PAS approval) are absent. Those that exist are credible but unproven.

C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

C6011th of 41

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

The Investment Analyst's heading The staircase of novel, dated positive developments ahead

Judgement: The staircase is real but its two most novel steps (PAS approval and PAS revenue) have slipped before, and the steps in the first six months are mostly confirmations of delivery rather than novelties. The most valuable novel step for the share price is a credible, beatable 2027 guide from the new chief executive combined with a dated PAS approval; the largest novel step over 18 to 36 months is PAS at scale plus a free cash flow inflection as capex falls.

Next 6 months (to about May 2027):

  • Swissmedic GMP certification for PAS (targeted within 2026; slipped from H2 2025 to Q1 2026 to "2026"). Novelty high (first proof of a new business model); foundation medium (repeated slips; non-GMP runs started in H1 2026).
  • FY 2026 results and first FY 2027 guidance, 23 March 2027. Novelty medium: proof that H2 2026 delivered about CHF 228m of sales and a 16% to 19% H2 margin, a 2027 guide from the new chief executive, H2 order intake (US onshoring wins), resolution of the CHF 15m to 20m cancellation item, and evidence that the S&C margin recovered in H2. Foundation medium to high (backlog).
  • Sector events: the BIOSECURE list of companies of concern (18-Dec-26), favouring Western equipment suppliers; peers' equipment-order commentary in January and February 2027. Novelty low for SKAN.
  • AGM (around May 2027): first grants under the share-based long-term incentive plan. Novelty low.

Months 6 to 18 (to about May 2028):

  • First PAS commercial revenue (2027) and first customer contracts. Novelty high; foundation medium.
  • H1 2027 results (August 2027): a less extreme H1/H2 split would be the first evidence of structurally better execution under the new chief executive. Novelty medium.
  • US onshoring equipment orders, mainly H2 2027 (Sartorius via Bernstein; Sector Digest), where SKAN reports no local high-end competitor and already won most large US projects in H1 2026. Novelty medium; foundation medium (Lonza: only about 5% of announced US investment has broken ground).
  • Restart of the CHF 40m GLP-1 project in 2027. Novelty low (expected).
  • FY 2027 results (March 2028): consensus EPS of 1.84 would exceed the 2024 peak of 1.73, a new high. Novelty medium.
  • A possible Capital Markets Day or recalibrated mid-term targets under the new chief executive. Novelty depends on direction; not dated.

Months 18 to 36 (to late 2029):

  • PAS approaching full capacity (about CHF 50m of revenue at up to 50% EBITDA margin, three-year ramp). Novelty high; foundation medium to low.
  • Free cash flow inflection as capex falls from 13% to about 7% of sales while EBITDA grows; possible deleveraging and higher payouts. Novelty medium.
  • US assembly facility (planned for 2027 to 2028) and S&C approaching the 50/50 target. Novelty low to medium.
  • Final resolution of the CFO case (appeal timing not given; could be within this window). Positive only in removing an overhang.
  • Negative steps in the same window: the 2027 to 2028 pharma capex plateau and the continuing turnkey share shift.

Reasoning: The near-term staircase is about proving delivery, which the market has partly priced (share price +48% in twelve months on multiple expansion). The genuinely novel positive steps sit in PAS and in cash conversion, and they are 6 to 36 months away with medium foundations. The investment case therefore needs patience through at least one more delivery test before the novel steps arrive.

Foundations

3

Value chain dynamics and industry structure

D4828th of 41

The Investment Analyst's group judgement: Industry and Value Chain

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

Industry and Value Chain

D4828th of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

SKAN leads a concentrated, regulation-protected niche whose demand is growing, but the niche is being absorbed into a larger market (the integrated fill-finish line) where SKAN is a mid-sized component supplier dependent on partners who are also its competitors. The value chain is moving profit away from the equipment stage toward validated consumables and mandatory service, and the fastest-growing buyers are the most price-conscious. The structure therefore supports volume and the complex, high-value end of the market, and works against share and price in standard lines. For the model: grow the isolator market at 8% to 10%, let SKAN's share in standard process isolators drift down, assume no real price in equipment, and lean the margin case on S&C and PAS rather than E&S pricing. For the share price, the sector's own pattern says the recovery is in the multiple and the next leg needs estimate upgrades.
