C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C6214th of 41

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

The Investment Analyst's heading Scope to improve financial outputs beyond cost cutting, peer precedents, and congruence with strategy

Judgement: Substantial scope, concentrated in three big areas rather than many small ones, all congruent with the stated strategy: (1) PAS, up to about CHF 20m to 25m of EBITDA at full capacity, worth 3 to 5 points of group margin on 2028 to 2029 sales; (2) E&S utilisation and standardisation, taking the full-year E&S margin from 4.3% (2025) toward the 12% to 15% that recent second halves already achieve; (3) S&C margin recovery from 20% toward 24% to 26% as service staff are absorbed and consumables grow. Smaller levers: pricing discipline in service (and possibly equipment under a Mettler-trained chief executive), material in-sourcing, digital service productivity. Together these make an upper-teens EBITDA margin by 2029 to 2030 achievable, but not with high probability, because each has slipped before.

Peer precedents: Getinge targets 16% to 19% from 14% by 2028, mainly by the mechanical fade of one-off quality and tariff costs; Tecan targets 20% from 16% by 2028 with two-thirds from cost-out; Sartorius holds about 30% through validated consumables; Syntegon reached 16.1% EBITDA at EUR 1.75bn of sales with a huge service base. SKAN's path is unusual in leaning on a new business model (PAS) for a large part of the uplift, which no peer has proven.

Modelling implication: Consensus (16.6% in 2028, 17.3% in 2029) already sits close to the mid-term target; the base case should assume PAS reaches roughly 30% to 50% of its CHF 50m potential by 2028, and treat full achievement as upside.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

D5220th of 41

Layer 1 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading Has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months?

Judgement: On a trailing basis yes, on the latest half-year no. Trailing-twelve-month sales to June 2026 were about CHF 363m, up about 9% on the prior twelve months (332m), well below SKAN's normal rate (a 17% to 19% CAGR from 2019 to 2024). But the engine is re-accelerating: H1 2026 sales grew 22.2% (24.0% at constant currency) and the backlog grew 27.8% in six months. The input force, order intake, is growing but more slowly than in 2024: trailing-twelve-month intake of about CHF 418m is up only about 4% on the prior twelve months (my derivation), although H1 2026 intake grew 16.3%. Regionally the input is uneven: Americas +49%, Europe +11%, Asia -56% (lumpy).

Reasoning: The 2025 deceleration was a conversion problem (customers not ready to take delivery), not an order problem; the order engine slowed in 2023 (-28% after the pandemic peak), recovered in 2024 (+22%) and has grown modestly since. The risk to watch is whether H2 2026 intake holds near H1's level, because consensus 2027 growth of about 15% needs it.

Modelling implication: Keep 2027 E&S growth tied to H2 2026 order intake; if H2 intake falls below about CHF 180m, cut 2027 sales growth toward 10%.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

F4832nd of 41

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

The Investment Analyst's heading Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, line by line, and how credible the gap is

Judgement: Consensus (effectively one model) projects SKAN back to its pre-2025 growth rate and beyond its historical margin peak by 2028, with free cash flow staying weak. The revenue path is credible for 2026 and 2027 given the backlog; the margin path is credible for 2026 to 2027 and depends on PAS and S&C for 2028 to 2029; two granular lines look light (depreciation and capex) and one looks heavy (dividend growth relative to cash).

Line by line (consensus against history):

  • Sales: +18.7% (2026), +14.8% (2027), +15.6% (2028), +20.3% (2029, one broker), against +15.5%, +12.9%, -7.7% in 2023 to 2025 and a 17% to 19% CAGR in 2019 to 2024. Credible for 2026 to 2027 (backlog 1.22x trailing sales, book-to-bill 1.5); 2028 to 2029 assume no pharma capex plateau and a PAS ramp. The 2029 acceleration to 20% looks like a single-broker artefact.
  • EBITDA margin: 14.1%, 15.4%, 16.6%, 17.3%, against 14.5%, 15.7%, 15.8%, 11.6% in 2022 to 2025. 2028 is the first year above SKAN's historical best. Credible only with PAS contributing (see C-53 for the segment decomposition) and S&C margin stabilised.
  • D&A: 12.0, 14.0, 16.0 against 13.2 in 2025. Looks light for 2026: about CHF 70m of PAS assets begin depreciating as the facility goes live, and capex has run at CHF 43m to 54m a year for three years.
  • EBIT and EPS: EBIT 41.0, 51.7, 66.2; EPS 1.40, 1.84, 2.39 (3.08 in 2029), against a peak of 1.73 (2024). The step from EBITDA to EPS implies a combined tax and minority leakage of about 19% to 20% of pre-tax profit, which is reasonable (tax 15% to 18%, minorities about 10% of net income less interest).
  • Cash: operating cash flow 41, 56, 71 (about 73% to 81% of EBITDA); capex 30.4, 33.0, 38.3 (8% to 7% of sales, against 11% to 14% historically); free cash flow 8, 18, 22. Capex looks low relative to the CHF 100m to 120m of mid-term growth capex guided at the 2025 Capital Markets Day; free cash flow is low relative to net income (26% to 44%).
  • Dividend: 0.36, 0.43, 0.53 per share, a 25% to 30% payout, consistent with policy.

Credibility judgement: The gap between consensus and history is credible on revenue because the change forces (Annex 1, ADC lines, onshoring) and the backlog support it. It is less credible on margin, because the historical evidence on SKAN's incremental margins in growth years is 17% to 24%, consensus needs about 24% to 25% for 2026 to 2028, and the S&C margin has been falling. In other words consensus is not heroic, but it leaves no room for another conversion slip, and it rests on one visible broker.

Forecasts and upgrades

–not yet rated

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

F428th of 9

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative together capture our assessment for the next six to twelve months almost exactly at the level of profit, and over-capture it beyond that. For 2026 and 2027 our EBITDA is the FactSet EBITDA; the company's 2026 guidance already says what we expect, and the first 2027 guide is more likely to be set at our numbers than above them. Inside that agreement there are two small differences of opposite sign: the sell-side has sales about 2% to 4% too high and margins about half a point too low. For 2028 and 2029 we are 8% and 18% below the sell-side on EBITDA and 15% and 24% below on EPS. The sell-side's growth for those years is the company's mid-term target carried forward; the wider sector's own debate (a plateau in customers' capital spending, onshoring arriving late, turnkey procurement) points to slower growth, and the brokers who follow that debate do not cover SKAN. The narrative has priced the recovery in volume in full: the share has risen 48% while estimates fell, the mean target is at the price, every rating is Buy, and on our earnings the multiple is already at its ten-year median.

My steer to the Consumer:

  • Against the test of whether expectations fail to capture probable developments: over the next six months, they capture them (a margin and cash flow somewhat better than expected, sales somewhat worse, net about neutral, with the two dates of 28 January and 23 March 2027 as the tests); over months 6 to 18, there is one probable positive that is not captured (the first half of 2027 on a flat cost base, which could produce the first upgrades to a current year) and one larger probable negative that is not captured (the 2028 estimates). This is not a set-up in which estimates are too low; it is one in which they are about right near term and too high further out.
  • On the sequence: the under-known positives arrive first and the under-known negatives later. That supports the narrative for another two reporting dates if the second half of 2026 is delivered, and argues against extrapolating that support into 2028.
  • On the return: the model's 18-month return at the guideline fair multiple (+26%; about +16% to +18% on my inputs, attributable EPS and dividends dated when paid) should be read with finding A3. Measured against the earnings the market capitalises today, our 2029 EPS is no higher, so nearly all of that return is a higher multiple on lower estimates. That has happened at SKAN in the last twelve months, but it needed the sector tide (D-07) and a narrative with only good news in it.
  • What would make the gap favourable: second-half 2026 order intake above 220 with United States projects (which would move our 2028 sales toward UBS's 508), a Swissmedic certification with a named customer, a quantified productivity programme, or a 2027 guide visibly below what a flat cost base can deliver. What would make it worse: a year-end slippage, order intake below 180, or a rating change in a market of two or three models.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F457th of 9

Layer 4 · Guidance

The Investment Analyst's heading How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events, and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, the mid-term targets.

Judgement: Over the next 18 months guidance is most likely to be met and not raised. SKAN has never raised guidance within a year, the window for 2026 has closed, and its pattern is to set a range at the full-year results, confirm it in August and warn late if it must. For 2026 the margin guide (13% to 15%) is very likely to be met in its upper half and has about a three-in-ten chance of being exceeded; the sales guide ("high-teens") is about as likely to be met as to be narrowly missed on a plain reading, and my base case (+16.1%) is the narrow miss. The first 2027 guide, on 23 March 2027, is more likely than not to sit below the FactSet sales figure and around the FactSet margin, which would make it the first SKAN guide set to be beaten; it lines up with our forecasts, not above them. The first event at which a raise is conceivable is the half-year report of August 2027 (about one in ten), and the more probable form of outperformance is a 2027 margin above the guided range (about three in ten), repeating 2023 and 2024. The mid-term targets (mid-to-upper-teens growth in both segments, a margin rising gradually to the upper teens, PAS at about CHF 50m) will be repeated in March 2027; I put about three in ten on their being re-dated or lowered within 18 months and better than even within three years, because our forecasts are below them (growth of 9% to 13%; a 17% margin only by 2029).

The events and my probabilities:

Event Outcome above In line Below
2026 sales against "high-teens" (preliminary figures 28-Jan-27, to be verified; results 23-Mar-27) above 19%: 5% 17% to 19%: 40% 15% to 17%, presented as close: 35%; under 15%: 20%
2026 EBITDA margin against 13% to 15% above 15%: 30% within: 60% (mostly the upper half) under 13%: 10%
First 2027 guide (23-Mar-27) against the FactSet figures (+14.8%, 15.4%) above: 10% brackets them: 35% below on sales, around on margin: 55%
Half-year report, August 2027 raised: 10% confirmed: 75% reduced: 15%
2027 outcome against the guide (warning window from November 2027; figures January to March 2028) above (margin more likely than sales): 30% within: 45% missed: 25%
2028 guide (March 2028) against the 2027 guide higher growth: 10% the same: 45% lower growth: 45%
Mid-term targets over 18 months raised: 5% repeated: 65% lowered or re-dated: 30%

What would drive each outcome:

  • 2026 sales. A plain meeting of the guide needs 390 to 397, which is second-half sales of 226 to 232 against my 222.5. There are two routes. E&S: converting 40% to 42% of the mid-year E&S backlog in six months, against 39% in my base and about 37% in the second half of 2025 (my estimates). S&C: the segment took 81.2 of orders in H1 2026 against 64.6 of sales and holds a service backlog of 79.8, so second-half S&C sales of 85 to 88, against the 82 in the base case, would by themselves close most of the gap. The route to a miss is the 2024 one: systems on site but customers' work unfinished at year-end. No warning by late November removes the 2025 route for most practical purposes (finding A5).
  • 2026 margin. Finding A1: the cost base is flat and headcount lower; a second half at the 18.9% of 2025 on 12% more sales would give 14.8% for the year, and SKAN exceeded the top of the same range in 2023 and 2024. The routes to the lower half are a sales shortfall, a provision on the item at risk, or PAS and bonus costs larger than I allow.
  • The 2027 guide. The chief executive confirmed and did not raise in August 2026 with orders up 16.3%; new managements after a miss tend to guide low (the AlphaSense report cites Stratec and SKF; Morgan Stanley called Sartorius's January 2025 guidance "deliberately cautious"); and the share-based incentive plans introduced in 2026 make their first grants around the 2027 annual general meeting (first set), which, on my conjecture, gives management no reason to set a high starting bar. Against that, he repeated the mid-term targets in August 2026, so the words are unlikely to be weaker than "mid-teens". The arithmetic then does the work: mid-teens growth (13% to 16%) on 2026 sales of 387 is 437 to 449, which has our 436 at its lower edge and the FactSet 454 above its top. Each CHF 1m by which 2026 falls short lowers the 2027 level implied by any growth guide by CHF 1.15m. On margin I expect the range to move up a point, to 14% to 16%, as it did between 2024 and 2025; our 16.0% is at its top and the FactSet 15.4% inside it.
  • August 2027. A raise would need a first half well above an ordinary one (an EBITDA margin of 14% or more against my 12.6% and 9.3% in H1 2026) together with a guide that had been set low; SKAN's habit and the weight of the second half argue for confirmation.
  • Late 2027. A miss needs one large project to move: the restart of the CHF 40m GLP-1 project slipping again (its restart date in 2027 is still undisclosed), the item at risk being cancelled, or a new postponement among the oncology and United States lines. A guide set at our level, not at the old ambitious level, is why I put the chance of a miss at one in four where SKAN's own record says one in two.
  • The 2028 guide and the mid-term targets. In the second set's order path (about 470 in 2027 after about 448 in 2026) the backlog at the end of 2027 supports about 11% growth in 2028. A 2028 guide of low-teens growth would be the first visible step down from "mid-to-upper teens" and the natural moment, or a capital markets day before it, to re-date the targets. The PAS target is the most exposed: its ramp starts at least a year later than planned in May 2025. The aspiration to triple sales and quadruple EBITDA has no date, came from the chief financial officer now under conviction, and I do not expect to hear it again.

How far guidance already reflects our assessment. For 2026, fully: we expect the margin guide met and the sales guide met or narrowly missed, with no raise possible. For 2027, the guide is likely to be set close to our numbers, so it will look conservative against the sell-side and not against us; the one place where we are above what guidance is likely to say is the margin. For the mid-term, guidance is above us. A record of never raising, a first guide that we expect to match our base case, and mid-term targets we do not expect to be met do not make a favourable gap; the modest offset is a margin that can beat its range.

I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

D387th of 9

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Over the next 18 months the sell-side's sales forecasts are more likely to be cut than raised, its margin forecasts more likely to be raised than cut, and its EPS forecasts for 2026 and 2027 to end roughly where they are or slightly lower; its forecasts for 2028 and 2029 are more likely than not to be cut. For the FactSet 2027 EPS figure of 1.84 I put about 25% on its being more than 3% higher in 18 months, 35% on its being within 3%, and 40% on its being more than 3% lower; for the 2028 figure of 2.39, about 15%, 30% and 55%. The sequence matters more than the totals: the January and March 2027 events should bring lower sales and higher margins (EPS about flat, breadth about nil); the August 2027 half-year report is the first event at which upgrades to the current year are more likely than downgrades, because the flat cost base shows most clearly against an easy first-half comparison; and the cuts to 2028 and 2029 come later, with the 2027 order intake and the 2028 guide. Net breadth means little here: the FactSet figures rest on two or three models, so one contributor moving is the whole revision.

Where the sell-side stands today:

  • Level. See the table above: our EBITDA is level with the FactSet figures for 2026 and 2027 on 2% and 4% less sales and a margin 0.4 and 0.6 points higher; our EPS is 2% and 6% lower; for 2028 and 2029 our EPS is 15% and 24% lower. My probability-weighted 2027 EPS (1.63) is 11% below the FactSet figure.
  • Momentum. For 2027: EPS revisions of 0%, 0%, +4% and -30% over one, three, six and twelve months; EBITDA -4%, -4%, +3%, -24%; sales -1%, -2%, 0%, -16%; the dashboard rates sell-side earnings momentum D (score -2). Estimates stopped falling six months ago and have not started to rise; in the last month EBITDA and sales were trimmed again while EPS did not move, which is the signature of a small number of models being adjusted, not of a trend.
  • Targets and ratings. The mean target price rose 9% in three months and 15% in six, to CHF 72, while estimates were flat: the sell-side has followed the share price by raising the multiple it applies. The mean target is 3% above the price of CHF 70.10 and UBS's CHF 77 is 10% above. Every visible rating is Buy. The sell-side therefore has no upside left to argue without raising estimates or the multiple again, and the only direction a rating can move is down.
  • A weakness inside the numbers. UBS's 2026 EPS of 1.54 on EBITDA of 57.8 implies that tax and minorities together take only about 14% of pre-tax profit (my arithmetic, using the brokers' depreciation and financial items), against about 20% on my inputs and 17.8% of tax alone in 2025. Its EPS is the more likely of the two contributors' to be cut at the 2026 results even if its EBITDA is right; the unnamed contributor's implied 1.26 errs the other way. The mean may not move, but the Consumer should expect the reported "consensus" for 2026 to be met at EBITDA and for at least one broker to be visibly wrong on EPS.

The likely revisions, event by event:

  • Preliminary figures (28-Jan-27, to be verified). 2026 sales of about 385 to 392 against 396: a trim of 1% to 3% to 2026 and, through the base, to later years. Order intake decides more: above about 220 for the half (with United States projects) would hold or raise 2027 and 2028 sales; about 200 would hold them; below about 180 would cut 2027 and 2028 by 3% to 5%.
  • Results and 2027 guide (23-Mar-27). 2026 EBITDA of about 55 to 58 against 55.9 and a margin of about 14.5% against 14.1%: a small beat on margin on lower sales. For 2027 I expect sales to move from 454 toward 440 to 445 (the base effect and a mid-teens guide), the margin from 15.4% toward 15.6% to 16.0% if the second-half margin and year-end headcount confirm the cost base, EBITDA to move by between -2% and +1%, and EPS by between -4% and +1%. For 2028 the lower base takes sales down 2% to 4% and EPS 3% to 5%. The single-broker 2029 figures (sales of 632, EPS of 3.08), which extrapolate growth of 17% to 20%, are the most likely to be rebased.
  • Half-year report (August 2027). If the first-half margin is 12.6% or better on sales up about 14%, and the cost base is still flat, I put 40% on 2027 EPS being raised, 35% on no change and 25% on a cut. This is the point at which finding A1 can become an estimate upgrade, because by then it will have been visible in three consecutive half-years.
  • January to March 2028. 2027 delivered at about our base (1.74) would be within a few per cent of where I expect the figure to be by then. The 2028 and 2029 estimates face their test here: if 2027 order intake is about 470 (the second set's path) and the 2028 guide steps down to low-teens growth, 2028 sales of 525 and EPS of 2.39 will not survive; I put 55% on a cut of more than 3% to 2028 EPS by then, and more for 2029.

Why, in one paragraph. The sell-side has its sales forecasts on the company's mid-term target and its margins on the old cost behaviour. Our assessment says the first is too high from 2028 (and slightly too high before that, on timing) and the second too low in 2026 and 2027. Because CHF 10m of sales is worth more than half a point of margin in EPS, the sales error dominates the totals; because the margin evidence arrives first, the early revisions should be benign.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. Cuts are likely: I put about two in three on the 2028 and 2029 EPS figures being lower three years from now than they are today, unless H2 2026 and 2027 order intake show United States onshoring and Annex 1 upgrades arriving faster than the second set assumes. The cash flow estimates are the exception: the FactSet free cash flow figures (8, 18, 22) are well below ours (about 15, 28, 28) and should be raised as capital spending of about 30 a year is reported, though this depends on customer advances holding.

What would change the direction to upgrades: H2 2026 order intake above 220 with United States wins; a Swissmedic certification accompanied by a named customer; a quantified productivity or pricing programme from the chief executive in March 2027; a second-half margin of 19% or more. And to deeper cuts: a year-end slippage in 2026, order intake below 180, cancellation of the item at risk together with further GLP-1 deferrals, or a rating change by UBS, which in a market of two or three models would be read as the consensus changing its mind.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0754
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C543rd of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: Day to day the share is overwhelmingly its own: over the latest six months the equal-weighted peer basket explains 16% of the variance of SKAN's daily returns (correlation 0.40, beta 0.80), and over 24 months the share explained has ranged between 7% and 35% and has usually been 15% to 25%. But the level tells a second story that the daily statistic hides: over the same six months SKAN rose 36.5% and the basket rose 31.9%, while SKAN's earnings estimates barely moved (2027 EPS up 4% in six months and down 30% over twelve). Almost all of SKAN's gain was multiple expansion, and it arrived while the whole group was re-rating. My reading is that the multiple is company-driven in its timing (it jumps on SKAN's own order and results news) and partly sector-driven in its level: I estimate that between a third and a half of the last six months' re-rating was the sector tide and so could be taken back by a sector rotation without any news from SKAN. The multiple is therefore more industry-dependent than a 16% figure suggests, though still mainly SKAN's own.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • The daily evidence. Correlation with individual peers is 0.11 to 0.30 (highest with Oxford Nanopore, Carl Zeiss Meditec and Sartorius; 0.23 with Tecan, the other Swiss name); no single peer explains more than 9%. The idiosyncratic share is 84%.
  • Why the daily figure understates the common element. SKAN trades about 38,000 shares a day (about USD 3m to 5m); thinly traded shares respond to common news over days, not within the day, which depresses daily correlation without removing the common factor (my conjecture: a weekly or monthly measure would be higher; the file holds daily data only). The explained share rose to 26% to 35% in April to September 2025 and again to 23% to 32% in March to August 2026, the two periods in which, on my reading, the sector as a whole was moving on a common cause (the spring 2025 market sell-off; the 2026 rotation into European healthcare), and fell back to 12% to 18% when it was not. SKAN's beta to the basket has also doubled, from 0.35 to 0.45 in early 2025 to 0.7 to 0.9 through 2026: it now takes more of each sector move than it did.
  • The level evidence. A beta of 0.8 on a basket return of 31.9% accounts for about 25 of SKAN's 36.5 points; the remaining 11 points are SKAN's own. Because estimates were flat, both parts are multiple. The Sector Digest's rule (European medtech rebounds through multiple expansion before analysts move) describes the basket as well as SKAN: most of the peers carry a sell-side momentum rating of C or worse while their prices rose.
  • The company-specific part is visible in the events. The 52-week low of about CHF 35.6 followed SKAN's own warning of 6 November 2025; the recovery to CHF 70 followed its own order data and the H1 2026 results; the P/E has ranged from 17.9x to 64.0x in five years, far wider than a sector factor would produce. On its own ten-year history SKAN's P/E is at the 32nd percentile; the peers range from the 6th (Carl Zeiss Meditec) to the 72nd, which again says each multiple is mainly its own.
  • What this means for predictability. A mainly company-driven multiple will move on SKAN's own two dates (late January and 23 March 2027), which is what an investor in a thesis wants; but a third to a half of the recent re-rating rests on a sector rotation that the first set's sources judge supportive for about three months and fading to neutral within twelve, and that part is outside the thesis. The first set's sentiment risks (C-82) are the channels: equipment-order comments from Danaher, Thermo Fisher, Repligen and Sartorius, all of which report before SKAN's full results.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

D5025th of 41

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading Historical precedents, rules of thumb and evidence on out- and under-performance in this sector, and SKAN's fit

Judgement: Four patterns from this sector and industry explain most share-price and operational outcomes, and SKAN fits each of them. The most decisive for the next 18 months is the last: once a recovery is in the multiple, further share-price gains need rising earnings estimates.

The patterns and SKAN's fit:

  1. Recurring revenue earns the multiple and the margin. Getinge (consumables and service 67% of sales), Sartorius (recurring about 75% to 80%), Tecan (consumables and service 62% of Life Sciences) show that validated consumables and mandatory service carry the economics; equipment carries thin margins and cyclicality. SKAN's S&C share is 35% (2025) to 39% (H1 2026), heading to a 50% target; the Wisdom Library's "asset-light services pivot" pattern suggests a re-rating once services pass about 25% of sales, which SKAN has passed. But the pattern also requires the services margin to be defended; SKAN's fell from 29.5% to 20.1%.
  2. Consumables recover first, equipment follows; equipment orders are hard to see. The Sector Digest's rule. SKAN is unusual: as an equipment maker it is already in its equipment upturn (Annex 1 and ADC lines), ahead of the bioprocess equipment cycle expected in H2 2027.
  3. Lumpy project companies are punished for guidance misses and rewarded for order surges. SKAN's own record: P/E five-year range 17.9x to 64.0x; the 2025 miss took the share to a 52-week low of CHF 35.6; the order recovery took it to CHF 70.1 (+48% over twelve months, +41% over six) while FY 2027 consensus EPS fell 30% over twelve months. The re-rating has been entirely multiple expansion. Precedents: Sartorius (2023 profit warning, then re-rated under a new chief executive who under-promised); Stevanato (de-rated on a single-customer GLP-1 reset); Tecan (rallied about 40% year to date into H1 2026, then sold off on flat Q2 orders despite an earnings beat).
  4. Diversifying into lower-margin adjacencies to smooth the cycle often amplifies it. Tecan bought Paramit (USD 1.0bn, 2021) to add a second engine; both engines contracted together and it impaired CHF 139.5m. SKAN's 2025 acquisitions are small by comparison, but the lesson applies: the S&C margin has fallen since they were added.

Rules of thumb that apply: European medtech rebounds through multiple expansion after a sell-off, before analysts move (Morgan Stanley via the Sector Digest); "slow growth is punished twice" (earnings and multiple); a market only has to stop shrinking for the multiple to recover.

Modelling implication for the third and fourth sets: The share price has priced the recovery to roughly consensus; the stock now needs estimate upgrades, which require H2 2026 delivery (sales about 228, EBITDA 37 to 44) and a credible 2027 guide on 23 March 2027.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

F3035th of 41

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: In a plausible bear case the fair trough is about CHF 40 to 45, 36% to 43% below CHF 70.1; a de-rating alone, with estimates intact, takes the shares to about CHF 55 to 57 (about -20%); a repeat of 2025 would justify about CHF 30 to 36 (about -50% to -57%). The 52-week low of CHF 35.6, set after the 2025 miss, is an empirical anchor for the severe case.

Reasoning and arithmetic:

  • Valuation anchors: the 24-month-forward P/E is 29.6x today (32nd percentile of five years); the ten-year tenth percentile ("crash" band) is 23.9x, the five-year low 17.9x. Implied 24-month-forward EPS is about CHF 2.37.
  • De-rating only: 23.9x times 2.37 is about CHF 57 (-19%); 20x is about 47 (-32%).
  • Moderate miss (2027 sales 5% below consensus; EPS about 1.42 for 2027 and about 1.8 to 1.9 for 2028, so a 24-month-forward EPS of about 1.8 to 1.9, since that measure is today almost entirely 2028): 23.9x gives about CHF 43 to 45 (-36% to -39%); 22x about 40 to 42.
  • Severe miss (a 2025-type year in 2027; EPS 0.85 to 1.0 for 2027 and about 1.5 for 2028): 20x to 24x on about 1.5 gives CHF 30 to 36 (-49% to -57%); a market that has just seen two collapses in three years is unlikely to pay more than the tenth-percentile multiple.
  • The Risks report's bear case (20x on EPS of 0.75) gives about CHF 15; I regard it as implausible as a trough given the record backlog and the installed-base revenue, but it shows what the bears believe.

Why the downside is wide: steep decremental margins, a P/E that has ranged from 18x to 64x in five years, illiquidity, and a share price whose 12-month gain came entirely from multiple expansion while estimates fell.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

C-5814th of 41

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative has already moved from "collapse" (2025) to "recovery confirmed by orders" (2026), and the share price has followed (+48% in twelve months, technical momentum A, relative strength in the 91st percentile, P/E at the 32nd percentile of five years). The next chapters ("delivery proven", then "PAS and cash conversion") are credible and dated within 6 to 36 months, but each depends on events that have slipped before, and the governance overhang will colour the narrative until the CFO appeal is resolved. The staircase supports the case only if the 23 March 2027 print and guide show reliable delivery; otherwise the narrative reverts quickly in a small, illiquid stock.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–62

Layer 3 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

Judgement: The two sets are congruent in their story and in most of their mechanics, and the story is a sound one: regulation and the order book give SKAN volume; margin has to be earned by the company; revenue should be leaned later rather than lower; the governance overhang and the share price having run ahead of estimates belong in the multiple, not in the forecasts. Where the sets jar, the cause is almost always the same: both carry the previous management's behaviour and the 2025 experience forward into the base case, while naming the new chief executive and the end of the investment phase as the main internal change forces. The jars are in numbers, not in logic; I have corrected the ones that touch inputs. None overturns the base case, but together they move the centre of the next 18 months from "revenue and profit both below consensus" to "revenue below, margin above, profit level", which is a different and more useful message for the memo.

What aligns (the joins that hold):

  • The first set's "volume, not price" (C-02, C-17, C-45) becomes the second set's zero real price in equipment and a capped E&S margin (P-04); the first set's "lean later, not lower" (C-61, C-69) becomes revenue 2%, 4% and 8% below the FactSet figures at an unchanged margin (P-06, P-09); the advances loop appears identically in both (C-52, P-02, P-05); both treat PAS as a separate, delayed, binary line; both put the governance overhang in the multiple.
  • Where the second set corrected the first, the new evidence supports the second set: the franc works through tender competitiveness rather than an immediate margin collapse (the AlphaSense currency report: most projects are priced in francs, foreign-currency contracts are hedged from signature, management's own sensitivity is 3% to 4% for a 10% move in the euro, and management reported no effect on order intake in the franc's 2022 surge); newer contracts carry price-adjustment clauses; consensus depreciation is read correctly as EBITDA less EBIT; minority leakage is about 4% of net income.

What jars, in order of consequence:

  1. The cost base (finding A1). The second set's base case has fixed costs growing about 10% in 2027 and calls cost discipline "the clearest source of upside"; its own evidence, read correctly, shows discipline is the present state. The first set scores the new chief executive as a moderate positive (C-57: 55; C-14: 54) and then models the predecessor's leverage (C-75). This is the largest inconsistency between the foundations and the forecasts, and it has a sign: upward on margin.
  2. The 2026 segment build does not sum to the 2026 group inputs (14.4% against 14.1%; second half 18.1% against 17.6%). Corrected in my inputs.
  3. The base case for 2026 sales is a small miss of the guidance as most readers will take it, presented as a meet. The second set calls +16.1% (387) "the bottom of high-teens". The AlphaSense guidance report reads "high-teens" as CHF 390m to 396m, and UBS has 399. On a plain reading (17% to 19%), our base case is CHF 3m to 9m short on sales while sitting in the upper half of the margin range. The memo should say so in those words; I-01 gives the probabilities.
  4. "Consensus is one model" (finding A2). It is at least two, and the second, unnamed one drives the outer years.
  5. Two EPS bases are in circulation (section C): the second set's text compares attributable EPS with consensus (-6%, -8%, -13%); the model compares EPS before minorities (-1.8%, -4.5%, -9.9%). The first is like for like.
  6. The cross-check's free cash flow and the inputs' working capital disagree for 2026 (section C). Corrected.
  7. The calendar (finding A4): both sets may be eight weeks late on the first test.
  8. Smaller items. The first set's franc sensitivity (15% to 25% of EBITDA) and "no indexation" stand corrected by the second set; the first set's 11% minority leakage likewise. The second set's statement that the 2026 set-up "resembles 2024 more than 2023" is fair on the order book but ignores the one way in which 2026 is unlike both: costs are not rising into the volume.

A point on which I think differently from both sets. Both describe the downside as a repeat of 2025 and weight it at about 15%. The mechanism of 2025 was specific: pandemic-era vaccine lines being deprioritised and one GLP-1 maker suspending a CHF 40m project. The vaccine lines are now largely worked through or already classed as delayed (about CHF 40m) or at risk (CHF 15m to 20m, excluded from planning), so the items that hurt in 2025 are identified and outside the plan. The remaining exposure is to new names in a backlog whose growth came from oncology and United States fill-finish lines. I keep the 15% because the cost structure makes any such event severe, but the memo should present it as "one large customer-controlled project moving a year" rather than as "2025 again".

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

B+482nd of 9

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

The Investment Analyst's heading How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of sell-side commentary and investor commentary, and how well known is each?

A caution on the evidence. I have no direct sight of the notes of the houses that cover SKAN. The AlphaSense narrative report names Jefferies, DNB Carnegie and Morgan Stanley as "the three covering houses", but those are brokers on SKAN's peers; it retrieved no SKAN research from UBS, Baader Helvea, Berenberg, Zürcher Kantonalbank, Research Partners, Vontobel or Kepler Cheuvreux, and the guidance report failed in the same way. No social-media commentary was retrieved at all. My reading therefore rests on what the sell-side has in its numbers (the FactSet figures, UBS's figures, targets and ratings), on what analysts asked on SKAN's calls as the reports relay it, on one fund's published reasoning (zCapital, 30-Sep-26), on the share price, and on expert calls. I mark confidence as medium to low and ask for the missing research (my second request).

Judgement: The narrative is carried by a handful of sell-side models and a small circle of Swiss small- and mid-cap investors, and it is a narrative about volume. What is widely known and already in the price is the order recovery, the record backlog, the regulatory and oncology drivers, the 2025 miss and its cause, the GLP-1 slowdown and the chief financial officer's conviction as a headline. What is known but neither analysed nor in the numbers is the S&C margin, PAS beyond its approval date, and the new chief executive's cost discipline. What is barely discussed at all is the effect on SKAN of a plateau in customers' capital spending after 2027, the loss of share to turnkey line builders, the role of customer advances, the free cash flow that follows the end of the investment phase, the accounting that flatters returns, and the fact that on realistic earnings the share is already at its historical median multiple. The under-known items do not all point the same way, and they do not arrive at the same time: the under-known positives (cost discipline and cash) will show in the next six to twelve months; the under-known negatives (2028 and 2029 growth, share, the pace of PAS, the multiple on our earnings) will show 12 to 36 months out. In size the negatives are the larger: half a point of margin is about 3% of EBITDA, while our 2028 EBITDA is 8% and our 2028 EPS 15% below the FactSet figures.

Each dynamic: who knows it, whether it is in the numbers, and where we stand

Reasoning on the pattern:

  • Why so much is under-discussed. Coverage is thin, the visible models anchor on the company's guidance and mid-term targets, the company reports twice a year, and the share's 48% rise has been explained to investors by one fact (orders). A narrative built on one fact has had no need of the others. This cuts both ways: under-known positives can persist unpriced until they are reported, and under-known negatives can arrive as surprises.
  • Two details that show how the debate is framed. Deutsche Bank's survey of injectable-drug specialists (13-Nov-26) names Syntegon, Bausch+Ströbel and Optima as the preferred vendors for fill-finish equipment; SKAN, the leader in the isolator that sits on those lines, does not appear, because the question asked was about the line. That is the turnkey issue in miniature, and it is being discussed without reference to SKAN. And the only investor reasoning in the files (zCapital) buys SKAN for profitability under the new chief executive, while the sell-side's 2027 margin is below 2024's: the investor narrative is slightly ahead of the sell-side's numbers on the one positive that is under-priced.
  • Peers and the sector. The sector narrative that investors do follow (consumables first, equipment later; onshoring orders in 2027; the Stevanato reset; the tools-sector capital spending debate) contains every piece of our 2028 and 2029 view; what is missing is its application to a small Swiss company that the sector analysts do not cover. The gap is one of attention, not of information, which makes it more likely to close on an event (a guide, an order number) than gradually.

Each layer's own material

What each layer was asked, what its scores mean, and the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 23 November 2026, 20:12 to 20:31 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH): First set of questions, foundational components of the investment case

Prepared 2026-11-23 for the Investment Analyst who builds the operating and financial model (second set). All money in CHF millions unless stated; per-share in CHF. Sources are the files listed in the instruction; where I derive a figure I say so.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation: reconciliations and derived figures used throughout (read before the answers)

These are the load-bearing numbers I rely on. Several resolve apparent contradictions in the research files.

  1. The backlog figures reconcile. The Risks report flags CHF 386.4m, 362.5m and 442.3m as "unreconciled". They are not: the group backlog at 30 June 2026 of 442.3 = Equipment and Solutions (E&S) backlog 362.5 + Services and Consumables (S&C) service backlog 79.8. Group backlog history: end-2022 360.1; end-2023 312.1; end-2024 318.3; mid-2025 386.4; end-2025 346.1; mid-2026 442.3 (+27.8% in six months). Backlog to last-twelve-month sales is 1.22x (442.3 / 363.2), against 0.88x at end-2024 and 0.98x at end-2023: the best forward cover since the post-pandemic peak.
  2. Segment EBITDA sums to group EBITDA; there is no unallocated corporate cost line. 2024: E&S 30.3 + S&C 26.7 = 57.0. 2025: 9.4 + 29.1 = 38.6. H1 2026: 2.3 + 13.0 = 15.3. Model the group as two segments only.
  3. Derived half-years by segment (my derivation from reported full-year and H1 figures). E&S: H1-24 sales 120.1, EBITDA 8.9 (7.4%); H2-24 150.8, 21.4 (14.2%); H1-25 90.7, -9.1 (-10.0%); H2-25 126.1, 18.5 (14.7%); H1-26 99.9, 2.3 (2.3%). S&C: H1-25 43.9, 10.0 (22.8%); H2-25 72.6, 19.1 (26.3%); H1-26 64.6, 13.0 (20.1%), of which 14.2 of sales is acquired (Metronik and ABC Transfer, consolidated from August 2025); organic S&C sales 50.4 (+14.8%).
  4. What the 2026 guidance requires of the second half (my arithmetic). Guidance is "high-teens" sales growth and a 13% to 15% EBITDA margin. For 16% to 19% growth, full-year sales are 387 to 397, so H2 sales must be 222 to 232 (+12% to +17% on H2 2025's 198.7). EBITDA of 50 to 60 means H2 EBITDA of 35 to 44, an H2 margin of 15.7% to 19.2%. The last three second halves delivered 17.5% (2023), 17.9% (2024) and 18.9% (2025). The low end of guidance therefore needs a weaker H2 margin than any of the last three years; the midpoint (about 17.4%) needs an ordinary one. The sell-side framing of an "extremely steep" second-half acceleration overstates the difficulty on margin; the real requirement is the H2 sales volume (about 228, the highest half-year in the company's history).
  5. Operating leverage, measured. Group decremental margins in the 2025 downturn: FY 2025 vs FY 2024, sales -28.0, EBITDA -18.4, a 66% decremental; H1 2025 vs H1 2024, a 71% decremental. Incremental margins in the growth years: FY 2023 23%, FY 2024 17% (management was adding 216 staff in 2023 and raising R&D to 8.3% of sales), H1 2026 48%. Consensus 2026 to 2028 implies a 24% incremental margin. The business carries steep downside leverage and, by management's choice, shallow upside leverage.
  6. Cash. FactSet free cash flow (operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex) 2021 to 2025: -39.3, +30.0, -30.6, -12.2, +12.0, a cumulative -40m against cumulative net income of about 130m (Viewforth definition) and dividends paid of 38.5m. Customer advances rose from 84.7 (end-2024) to 123.8 (end-2025) and 132.2 (mid-2026): roughly 39m of 2025's 64.1m operating cash flow came from advances, not earnings. The 2025 acquisitions (about 85m to 90m, derived from net investing cash flow of -131m less capex 43m) were funded by new bank debt (total debt 10.6 to 131).
  7. Consensus is one visible broker. FactSet consensus (UBS is the only named contributor): 2026 sales 396, EBITDA 55.9 (14.1%), EPS 1.40; 2027 454, 70.1 (15.4%), 1.84; 2028 525, 87.2 (16.6%), 2.39; 2029 632, 109 (17.3%), 3.08. Five houses cover the stock (UBS, Baader Helvea, Berenberg, Zürcher Kantonalbank, Research Partners, plus Vontobel and Kepler Cheuvreux named), but their models are not in the data.
  8. Valuation at CHF 70.1 (23-Nov-26). Market capitalisation about 1,558; enterprise value 1,612 (FactSet, incl. debt 131, cash 88.5, minorities 11.8). P/E on consensus: 50x 2026, 38x 2027, 29x 2028. EV/EBITDA: 29x 2026, 23x 2027, 18.5x 2028. 24-month-forward P/E 29.6x, 32nd percentile of five years; ten-year bands: 10th percentile 23.9x, median 32.7x, 90th 44.7x.

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: External change forces · Group: Internal change forces · Group: Industry and Value Chain · Group: Company - Competitive advantage · Group: Company - Economic strength · Group: Company - Economic strength (continued) · Group: Company - Management · Group: Risks · Group: Guidance · Group: Sell-side estimates · Group: Stock market narrative

Notes the Investment Analyst wrote inside its groups

Inside the group Industry and Value Chain (Layer 1)

A framing that runs through this group: SKAN competes in two markets at once. In the isolator market (the barrier system around a filling line, plus laboratory, sterility-testing and cell-therapy isolators) it is the global leader. In the fill-finish line market (filler plus isolator plus decontamination plus automation, bought increasingly as one package) it is a component supplier to, and competitor of, much larger filling-machine makers. Most answers below differ depending on which market is meant, and the trend is that the second market increasingly decides the first.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH), a Swiss maker of isolators and aseptic process systems for sterile (aseptic and aseptic-toxic) drug manufacturing, reporting two segments: Equipment & Solutions (custom and standard isolators, integrated fill-finish process systems with partner filling machines, eBeam and H2O2 decontamination, laboratory and cell-therapy isolators) and Services & Consumables (requalification, maintenance, retrofits and spare parts for the installed base; consumables from Aseptic Technologies closed vials and ABC Transfer sterile transfer bags and ports; Metronik manufacturing execution software; and the Pre-Approved Services pay-per-use filling business run through Maeven AG)

Request: T-P1

Peers: Syntegon Technology (private, CVC and Apollo owned; the largest fill-finish line maker, now offering its own isolators through Telstar and the SynTiso gloveless line, the main turnkey rival); Optima Packaging Group (private; turnkey filling lines with in-house isolators, a direct rival for process isolators); Groninger and Bausch+Ströbel (private filling-machine makers that partner with SKAN and also build isolators); Getinge (listed; Life Science isolators such as ISOPRIME and ISOFLEX and DPTE BetaBags, a rival in isolators and in sterile transfer consumables); Stevanato Group (listed; engineering and fill-finish equipment alongside containment, with a recent capacity-ramp margin drag); Tecan Group (listed Swiss instrument maker with a Swiss franc cost base and a dated cost programme, "Rewired"); GEA Group (listed process-equipment maker with project-based revenue); Comecer (isolators, part of ATS Corporation) Inputs: What is SKAN's split between fixed and variable costs, given personnel expenses of 50.2% of net sales in 2025 and 55.3% in H1 2026 (company reports) and material and external services of 24.3% of net sales in H1 2026 (H1 2026 interim report, 18 Aug 2026)? How did Equipment & Solutions EBITDA respond to volume in each half-year from 2023 to H1 2026 (company-reported segment EBITDA was -9.1m on 90.7m of sales in H1 2025, 9.4m on 216.8m for FY 2025, and 2.3m on 99.9m in H1 2026), and how much of each half-year swing came from percentage-of-completion phasing (engineering versus production phases) rather than from volume? What did the headcount additions of 216 in 2023 and 284 in 2025 (208 by acquisition) add to the fixed cost base, and what is the cost per employee in Switzerland compared with Germany, Belgium, France and Slovenia? What does the Pre-Approved Services facility (planned investment of about CHF 70m, CFO, earnings calls) cost to run before revenue, in which segment are those costs booked, and what utilisation is needed to break even? What explains the fall in the Services & Consumables EBITDA margin from 29.5% (2024) to 25.0% (2025) and 20.1% (H1 2026): the margins of Metronik and ABC Transfer, service headcount build-up, Pre-Approved Services start-up costs, or timing as management said on 18 Aug 2026? Which costs proved cuttable in the 2025 downturn and in peers' downturns (R&D and strategic initiatives were 8.3% of sales in 2024; other operating expenses fell 9.5% in H1 2026; temporary staff were used in 2023), and did SKAN or any peer cut engineering staff and then struggle when demand returned? What has standardisation (VARIOSYS, modular components, pre-production of standard isolators in 2025) delivered in engineering hours and margin? What efficiency or margin targets, if any, has the new chief executive Jonas Greutert attached to the segment-accountability structure introduced in 2026? How did margins of fill-finish and isolator equipment makers move with volume in 2009-2011, 2020-2022 and 2023-2025, and what did Syntegon's EBITDA expansion in 2025 and Tecan's "Rewired" savings actually deliver against what was promised? Focus: How much of each extra franc of Equipment & Solutions and Services & Consumables revenue reaches EBITDA in 2027 to 2029 given the capacity built in 2021 to 2025, how much management is likely to reinvest, and how severe the decremental margin would be in another year of customer postponements; and whether the Services & Consumables margin decline is structural or temporary.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Syntegon Technology (private; turnkey lines with its own isolators, the reference for bundled pricing); Optima Packaging Group (private; turnkey rival whose staff say SKAN's isolators carry a premium); Groninger and Bausch+Ströbel (private; partners and potential isolator rivals in joint bids); Getinge (listed; ISOPRIME isolators and DPTE BetaBags, the direct comparison for ABC Transfer consumables pricing); Tofflon Science and Technology (listed in China; low-cost aseptic equipment); Esco (private; reportedly winning sterility-testing isolator contracts in North America on price); Stevanato Group (listed; containment and fill-finish equipment with exposure to GLP-1 customers); Sartorius (listed; a reference for price increases on validated consumables) Operating conditions: the post-pandemic order slump and vaccine and GLP-1 project postponements of 2023-2025; the shift from allocation to competitive tenders once supply and demand reached equilibrium; turnkey procurement where filling-machine makers supply their own isolators; price-conscious buyers (contract manufacturers, generic and biosimilar makers); input-cost inflation on fixed-price multi-year contracts; US import tariffs (EU-origin rates, Delivered-at-Place terms); a strengthening Swiss franc against the euro and the dollar; Annex 1-forced upgrades and US onshoring greenfield projects; the aftermarket for requalification, spare parts and consumables Inputs: What price increases has SKAN achieved and held on spare parts, requalification services and consumables since 2022 (management described increases accepted without pushback, Q2 2025 earnings call, 19 Aug 2025), and has it achieved any like-for-like price increase on equipment? Former chief executive Thomas Huber said SKAN isolators can be up to 30% and sometimes 50% more expensive than competitors' (Q4 2023 earnings call, 26 Mar 2024): how has that premium moved since tenders became competitive, and what is SKAN's quotation success rate by product type (about 50% overall, annual report)? How are SKAN's project contracts structured (fixed price, milestones, customer advances, indexation, price addenda) and how were 2021-2023 cost inflation and 2025 US tariffs handled, including customer requests that SKAN absorb duties? In turnkey bids, how do Syntegon, Optima, Bausch+Ströbel and Groninger price an in-house isolator against a third-party SKAN isolator, and what discounts accompany bundling? An Optima expert said average project values rose 15% to 20% a year on regulatory complexity (expert call, 29 Jul 2025): how much of that is scope and how much price? How do ABC Transfer bags and ports and Aseptic Technologies closed vials compare in price with Getinge DPTE BetaBags and other alternatives, and are they repriced annually? How is Pre-Approved Services priced, and what have prospective customers said about willingness to pay? Has SKAN passed franc strength into euro and dollar prices, and how did pricing behave in previous periods of franc appreciation (2011, 2015, 2023)? Does the new chief executive, formerly at Mettler-Toledo, plan a pricing programme? Focus: Whether SKAN can raise real prices in services and consumables through 2029 and hold its equipment premium against turnkey bundlers and price-conscious buyers, and in which products, customer groups and currency conditions its pricing power fails.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: SKAN's medium-term outcome depends on whether the reinforcing loop from its growing installed base (mandatory requalification, spares, validated consumables) outweighs the loop working against it, in which every turnkey line delivered with a filling-machine maker's own isolator strengthens that maker's next bid, and on a cash loop tied to customer advances. Peers: Syntegon Technology (private; the turnkey loop's main beneficiary); Optima Packaging Group, Bausch+Ströbel and Groninger (private; partners and rivals whose choices decide the loop); Getinge (listed; an installed-base and sterile-transfer consumables loop in Life Science); Sartorius (listed; the regulatory-embedding consumables annuity loop); Tecan Group (listed; a precedent where a diversifying acquisition amplified the cycle instead of smoothing it); Stevanato Group (listed; GLP-1 capacity and customer-concentration loop) Inputs: How fast does SKAN's installed base (about 1,100 to 1,150 isolators, company presentations) generate requalification, spare-parts and retrofit revenue per unit per year, how long does each unit's aftermarket last, and how is the service backlog (CHF 79.8m at 30 June 2026) built? An Optima regional sales manager said SKAN's process-isolator share fell from about 70% to about 50% in two years as filling-machine makers developed their own isolators (expert call, 29 Jul 2025), while SKAN says it won most larger US fill-finish projects in H1 2026 (earnings presentation, 18 Aug 2026): which is right, by segment, and is the turnkey loop accelerating? How strongly do customer advances (CHF 123.8m at end-2025 and CHF 132.2m at 30 June 2026, company reports) feed operating cash flow and investment, and what happened when orders fell in 2023 (operating cash flow of CHF 8.7m, annual report)? Does capacity built ahead of demand create a balancing loop through under-absorption and investment cutbacks? Does SKAN's role in training regulators (nearly 700 inspectors at Allschwil) and drafting ISPE containment guidance feed back into specifications and orders? Will early Pre-Approved Services customers create a reinforcing adoption loop, or will delays slow it? Could GLP-1 capacity overbuild (Stevanato's 2027 outlook, Jefferies, 18 Nov 2026) and a 2027 large-pharma capex peak (Deutsche Bank, 15 Oct 2026) start a balancing loop of postponements and cancellations? Focus: Which loops will dominate SKAN's revenue and margins from 2027 to 2030, and whether the installed-base and consumables loop is strong enough to lift Services & Consumables growth and margins before the turnkey share loop and a capex plateau erode Equipment & Solutions.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: SKAN's EPS growth depends on below-EBITDA items (depreciation as new capacity goes live, minorities, tax, interest, currency) and on an accounting basis (Swiss GAAP FER with goodwill offset against equity) that the files do not quantify, and sell-side coverage visible in FactSet is a single broker. Peers: Stevanato Group (listed; fill-finish equipment and capacity-ramp depreciation drag); Tecan Group (listed; Swiss franc reporting with unhedged translation); Getinge (listed; currency, tariffs and adjusted-versus-reported earnings); Sartorius (listed; minorities and an EBITDA-to-EPS bridge); GEA Group (listed; project equipment cash conversion); Syntegon Technology (private; scale and margin reference) Inputs: What depreciation will follow as the Pre-Approved Services facility (planned investment of about CHF 70m) and the capex of 2023 to 2025 (CHF 35.3m, 53.8m and 45.9m, annual reports) go into service, and over what useful lives? What share of profit goes to minority holders of Metronik (76% owned), ABC Transfer (84%) and Aseptic Technologies (90%), and are there put or call options on the remaining stakes, at what prices and with what accounting? What is the outlook for SKAN's tax rate (12.8% in 2024 and 17.8% in 2025, FactSet) under Pillar Two and cantonal changes? What are the terms of the bank loans of about CHF 131m that funded the 2025 acquisitions (rates, maturities, covenants) and what interest does cash earn? Under Swiss GAAP FER SKAN offsets goodwill against equity: what goodwill was offset for Metronik and ABC Transfer, and what theoretical amortisation does the note disclose? Did the release of a CHF 16.1m project-related inventory allowance in 2023 (risks research) pass through the income statement? What are SKAN's currency sensitivities by currency for sales, costs and EBIT, its hedge ratios and horizon, and what do brokers assume for EUR/CHF and USD/CHF in 2027? How large and how fast is the Pre-Approved Services revenue ramp expected to be (management: about CHF 50m at full capacity with EBITDA margins up to 50%, ramp of about three years from commercial start), and what do UBS, Baader Helvea, Berenberg, Zürcher Kantonalbank, Research Partners, Vontobel and Kepler Cheuvreux assume for it and for depreciation, minorities, tax and the share count (new share-based incentive plans from January 2026)? Focus: How much of the growth from consensus EBITDA to consensus EPS for 2026 to 2028 is secured and how much is at risk from depreciation, minorities, tax, currency and the timing of Pre-Approved Services revenue, and where individual brokers' assumptions differ.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 24 November 2026, 14:17 to 14:30 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH): Second set of questions, the operating and financial model

Prepared 2026-11-24 for the Investment Analyst who answers the third and fourth sets. Money in CHF millions unless stated; per-share figures in CHF. "Consensus" means the FactSet figures in the financials block of 19-Nov-26, which rest on one visible broker (UBS, 29-Sep-26); I write "consensus" for brevity but it is one model. Where I derive a figure I say so. My forecast inputs are in forecast-inputs.md; the numbers below are the same numbers.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation: my base case in one table, and five points where I differ from the first set

Net debt (cash) at year end (51.1) 37.4 29 19 6 9 (22)

The one-sentence version: my margins match consensus, but my revenue is 4% to 8% lower in 2027 to 2028 (later conversion, a 2028 order plateau and a slower Pre-Approved Services ramp) and my below-EBITDA leakage is slightly higher, so my EPS is 6% below consensus for 2026, 8% below for 2027 and 13% below for 2028; on my numbers SKAN regains its 2024 EPS (1.73) only in 2028, four years after the peak.

Where I differ from the first set, and why (each is used below):

  1. Consensus depreciation is not light. The first set read the block's separate D&A line (12.0, 14.0, 16.0) and called it light. That line is inconsistent with the same block's consensus EBITDA and EBIT: EBITDA minus EBIT gives 14.9 (2026), 18.4 (2027) and 21.0 (2028). The broker already depreciates the Pre-Approved Services (PAS) facility; my own D&A (13.6, 17.5, 19.5) is, if anything, slightly lower. Depreciation is not a source of downside to consensus EPS.
  2. Pillar Two does not apply to SKAN. The OECD global minimum tax applies to groups with consolidated revenue of at least EUR 750m; SKAN's is about CHF 333m to 390m. The AlphaSense EPS-bridge report's Pillar Two worry is therefore misplaced. The real tax drivers are profit mix (low-tax Basel-Landschaft against German, Slovenian, French and Belgian rates) and the running-off of tax-loss carryforwards (CHF 3.3m used in 2024, 1.0m in 2025). I model 17.5% falling to 16.5%, not the 21.7% "expected rate", because the profit recovery is concentrated in the Swiss E&S business.
  3. Minority leakage is about 4% of net income, not about 10% to 11%. ABC Transfer is fully consolidated with no minority interest because of a mutual, irrevocable agreement to buy the remaining 16% (booked as a discounted liability); only Metronik (24%) and Aseptic Technologies (10%) leak. The H1 2026 ratio (0.6 of 5.4, 11%) is a seasonal artefact: the acquired businesses earn evenly through the year while group profit is concentrated in the second half. I model minorities of about 1.2 to 1.8 a year, falling to about 0.5 after a 2029 buy-out of Metronik's minority.
  4. The franc hits SKAN through price, not through an immediate margin collapse. Most capital projects are contracted in Swiss francs and the foreign-currency minority is hedged from contract signature (former CEO, 26-Mar-24). The first set's estimate that a sustained 10% franc appreciation would cut EBITDA by 15% to 25% once hedges roll off overstates the near-term effect: the franc cost base is passed to customers in the contract currency, so a stronger franc shows up as weaker competitiveness in tenders against euro-priced rivals and as translation of the foreign service businesses, over 12 to 36 months. My estimate is 4% to 8% of EBITDA over two years for a sustained 10% move, with the larger risk being lost tenders rather than lost margin.
  5. Equipment contracts are partly indexed since 2022. The first set modelled no indexation on equipment backlog. Management said in August 2022 that new contracts regularly contain inflation clauses, after raising prices by about 7% and then 15%; a minority of customers refused. Older backlog is therefore less exposed to cost inflation than the first set implied, though tender pricing since 2024 limits how much of this protection survives.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

SKAN reports two segments; segment EBITDA sums to group EBITDA (no unallocated corporate line). Pre-Approved Services (PAS, run through Maeven AG) is booked in Services & Consumables (S&C).

Half-years for 2026: E&S H1 99.9 at 2.3% (reported), H2 140.5 at 15.0%; S&C H1 64.6 at 20.1% (reported), H2 82.0 at 23.5%; group H1 164.5 at 9.3% (reported), H2 222.5 at 17.6%. 2026 S&C growth includes about seven extra months of Metronik and ABC Transfer (consolidated from August 2025, about CHF 28m to 30m of annual sales); organic S&C growth is about 12% to 15%.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • E&S revenue is driven by backlog conversion, not orders. Group backlog was a record CHF 442.3m at 30-Jun-26 (E&S 362.5, service 79.8), 1.22x trailing sales. I convert 55% to 65% of opening E&S backlog within twelve months plus in-year standard isolators, assume H2 2026 order intake of about CHF 200m (H1 247.6), about 470 in 2027, a plateau at about 480 in 2028 as large-pharma capex peaks in 2027 (Deutsche Bank, 15-Oct-26), and recovery from 2029 on US onshoring and Annex 1 upgrades. I lean conversion later than consensus because 2024 and 2025 both saw customer-driven postponements (about 30 projects at end-2024; a CHF 40m GLP-1 project moved to 2027; CHF 15m to 20m of backlog at risk of cancellation and excluded from planning). E&S share drifts down 1 to 2 points a year in standard lines (turnkey rivals), offset by rising project values in complex ADC, eBeam and aseptic-toxic lines.
  • S&C revenue is driven by the installed base (over 1,100 isolators, mandatory requalification, spare parts, retrofits), consumables (Aseptic Technologies closed vials, ABC Transfer transfer bags), Metronik software, and price of +2% to +4% a year. Organic growth 12% (2027), 11% (2028), 10% (2029 to 2030).
  • PAS: Swissmedic approval assumed in H1 2027 (it has slipped three times); revenue 5, 14, 24, 32 (2027 to 2030) against management's about CHF 50m at full capacity, three years from commercial start, at up to 50% EBITDA margin. I assume the PAS margin reaches about 40%. PAS assets (about CHF 70m) start depreciating around Q2 2027 at about CHF 5m a year.
  • Margins: cost base is about 75% fixed within a year (personnel 50.2% of 2025 sales); materials 24% to 25% of sales. Incremental EBITDA margin of about 26% to 27% in 2027 and 2028 (at the top of SKAN's growth-year history of 17% to 23%, consistent with H1 2026 cost discipline but below its 48% H1 2026 incremental). E&S margin capped near the 2024 level (11.2%) by zero real equipment pricing; S&C margin recovers from 20.1% (H1 2026) to about 23.5% in H2 2026 and 24% by 2029, not to the 29.5% of 2024, because acquired businesses and PAS carry different economics. H1/H2 seasonality persists (H1 margins 12% or lower, H2 17.5% to 18.5%).
  • Currency: most capital projects are priced in Swiss francs and the foreign-currency minority is hedged from signature; translation of foreign service businesses is unhedged. Reported growth assumed 1 to 1.5 points below constant currency at EUR/CHF about 0.90 to 0.92 and USD/CHF about 0.78 to 0.82 (Morgan Stanley and ING forecasts for 2027).
  • Depreciation: buildings 40 years, machinery 3 to 10 years; D&A flat at about 13 in 2023 to 2025 despite heavy capex because PAS assets were not in service; H1 2026 D&A 6.6.
  • Net financial items: CHF 120m syndicated loan at 0.95% (SARON plus margin), EUR loans of CHF 7.1m at 1.25% to 3.00%, near-zero interest on cash, foreign-exchange results and the unwinding of the ABC Transfer liability; falling as net debt falls.
  • Tax: 17.8% in 2025 on a profit mix tilted away from low-tax Switzerland; easing as Swiss E&S profit recovers and PAS (Swiss) profits arise. Pillar Two does not apply (consolidated revenue below EUR 750m).
  • Dividend: 30% payout of attributable EPS (policy kept in 2025: CHF 0.22 on EPS of 0.72); shown for the fiscal year it is declared for, paid the following spring.
  • Share count: 22.5m shares; share-based incentive plans from January 2026, delivered from treasury shares bought in the market (CHF 0.3m in H1 2026); about 20,000 shares a year delivered; no buyback programme.
  • Working capital (read this before using the line): the model's history uses FactSet's net working capital (receivables plus inventories less payables: CHF 128m, 38.5% of revenue at end-2025; 118m, 32.5% at mid-2026), which excludes customer advances (CHF 132.2m at mid-2026). On the company's definition, which includes advances, net working capital was -26.6 (end-2025) and -30.5 (mid-2026). My economic view is company-basis working capital of -8% of revenue in 2026 and 2027, -7% in 2028 and -6.5% in 2029 and 2030 (advances growing with the backlog, then flattening as orders plateau). The percentages entered are on FactSet's basis and are set so that the year-on-year change in the balance reproduces the cash effect of that view (inflows of about 4.4, 3.9, -1.0, 0.4 and 3.1 in 2026 to 2030); they fall as a share of revenue only because rising advances are netted inside them. If orders fall, advances unwind and these figures would be too low by CHF 10m to 15m a year.
  • Capex: 2026 consistent with CHF 13.7m in H1; 7% to 8% of sales thereafter, including a US assembly facility in 2027 to 2028; within the CHF 100m to 120m mid-term growth capex guided at the May 2025 Capital Markets Day.
  • Lease payments: under Swiss GAAP FER operating lease rent sits inside EBITDA; only finance leases (about CHF 4m in FactSet's debt) are paid outside it, about CHF 1m a year.
  • Acquisitions less disposals: no new M&A in the base case (management is not mooting deals; leverage headroom is modest). FY2027: about CHF 3m for the remaining 16% of ABC Transfer (a mutual, irrevocable purchase commitment booked as a discounted liability; timing and price not disclosed, my estimate). FY2029: about CHF 25m for Metronik's 24% minority under put and call options exercisable from 2029 at fair market value (my estimate from goodwill of CHF 75.5m on the 76% stake; amount and timing uncertain).
  • Minorities (not an input line; the model does not deduct them unless it has its own line): about 1.2 (2026), 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, then about 0.5 in 2030 after the Metronik buy-out; ABC Transfer has no minority line; Aseptic Technologies 10%. My EPS cross-check deducts them; a model that does not will show EPS about CHF 0.05 to 0.08 higher than mine.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

  • 2026 guidance (set March 2026, confirmed 18-Aug-26): "high-teens" net sales growth and an EBITDA margin of 13% to 15%. My inputs: +16.1% (the bottom of high teens; revenue CHF 387m against about 387 to 397 for 16% to 19%) and 14.1% (the middle of the range). The difference from the guidance midpoint (about +17.5%, CHF 391m) is H2 2026 conversion: I assume H2 revenue of 222.5 (+12%), the highest half-year in SKAN's history but below the about 226 to 232 the midpoint needs, because the set-up resembles 2024, when about 30 projects slipped at year-end.
  • Mid-term targets (Capital Markets Day, 15-May-25, reiterated): mid-to-upper-teens net sales growth in both segments; EBITDA margin gradually rising to the upper teens; S&C toward 50% of sales; PAS about CHF 50m of revenue at up to 50% EBITDA margin; an aspiration to triple sales and quadruple EBITDA (no date). My inputs are below the growth target (12.7%, 11.0%, 8.8%, 9.4% for 2027 to 2030) because of the 2028 capex plateau, share drift in standard equipment and a slower PAS ramp; my margin reaches 17.0% to 17.3% in 2029 to 2030, the low end of "upper teens"; S&C reaches 44% of revenue by 2030, not 50%. I treat the mid-term targets as the bull case.
  • No 2027 guidance yet: the first formal guide comes with FY 2026 results on 23-Mar-27, from a chief executive who confirmed rather than raised 2026 guidance despite strong orders. I expect a beatable guide (low-to-mid-teens growth, further margin improvement), consistent with my inputs rather than with consensus (+14.8%, 15.4%).
  • Consensus for reference (FactSet, one visible broker, UBS): revenue 396, 454, 525 (2026 to 2028); EBITDA margin 14.1%, 15.4%, 16.6%; EPS 1.40, 1.84, 2.39 (3.08 for 2029). My margins match; my revenue is 2%, 4% and 8% lower; my EPS 6%, 8% and 13% lower.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • EBITDA: the company's reported EBITDA (Swiss GAAP FER), FactSet's operating EBITDA and the brokers' adjusted EBITDA are the same figure in the base year (CHF 38.6m in 2025 on all three). My EBITDA margins are on that basis. Because Swiss GAAP FER has no IFRS 16, operating lease rent is inside EBITDA; IFRS peers' EBITDA is higher by their rent, so SKAN's margin understates its position against IFRS peers by roughly 1 to 2 points (my estimate; rent is not disclosed in the files).
  • EPS: brokers' adjusted EPS equals reported EPS attributable to shareholders over basic shares (2025: brokers' 0.720 against reported 0.724). There are no adjustments to make: no amortisation of acquired intangibles passes through the income statement, because goodwill (CHF 86.8m for Metronik and ABC Transfer in 2025) is offset against equity at acquisition. The theoretical five-year amortisation disclosed in the notes (CHF 8.3m in 2025; about CHF 17m to 18m a year in 2026 to 2029 on my estimate) is excluded on both the reported and the brokers' basis; it matters only when comparing SKAN with peers' unadjusted IFRS figures or computing returns on capital including acquisitions.
  • Depreciation in consensus: the block's separate consensus D&A line (12.0, 14.0, 16.0) is inconsistent with consensus EBITDA less consensus EBIT (14.9, 18.4, 21.0 for 2026 to 2028). The latter is the broker's actual depreciation assumption and already includes the PAS facility; compare my D&A (13.6, 17.5, 19.5) with it, not with the separate line.
  • Below EBIT: consensus implies net financial items of about -2.4, -1.4 and -0.9 (EBIT less pre-tax profit) and a combined tax and minority leakage of about 18% to 19% of pre-tax profit; mine are -3.5, -2.5, -2.0 and about 20%.
  • Free cash flow: FactSet's history defines free cash flow as operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex only; my cross-check deducts all capex including intangibles and lease payments, so it is about CHF 2m to 4m a year lower than FactSet's definition would give on the same cash flows. Consensus free cash flow (8, 18, 22) is FactSet's estimate on its own definition.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years
