Earnings per share 1.32 1.69 2.08 2.38 2.78
Free cash flow 14 24 26 38 48

EPS is net income attributable to shareholders (after minorities of 1.2, 1.4, 1.6, 1.8 and 0.5) over 22.5m to 22.6m basic shares: net income attributable 29.7, 38.1, 46.9, 53.8, 62.7. Free cash flow is operating cash flow (about 45, 59, 65, 75, 87) less capex including intangibles and lease payments, before acquisitions and dividends. Operating cash flow assumes customer advances keep rising with the backlog through 2027; in a year of falling orders it would be CHF 10m to 15m lower.

Company: SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH), a Swiss maker of isolators and aseptic process systems for sterile drug manufacturing, reporting two segments: Equipment & Solutions (custom and standard isolators, integrated fill-finish process systems built with partner filling-machine makers, eBeam and H2O2 decontamination, laboratory, sterility-testing and cell-therapy isolators) and Services & Consumables (requalification, maintenance, retrofits and spare parts for the installed base; Aseptic Technologies closed vials; ABC Transfer sterile transfer bags and ports; Metronik manufacturing execution software; and the Pre-Approved Services pay-per-use filling business run through Maeven AG)

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 25 November 2026, 11:25 to 11:57 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH): Third set (predictability of the forecasts over 18 months) and fourth set (what guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative already reflect)

Prepared 2026-11-25 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money in CHF millions unless stated; per-share figures in CHF. E&S is the Equipment and Solutions segment; S&C is the Services and Consumables segment; PAS is the Pre-Approved Services pay-per-use filling business (Maeven AG), reported inside S&C. "The FactSet figure" is the FactSet median in the financials block of 19-Nov-26. "Attributable EPS" is EPS after minority interests, which is the basis the brokers use. Where I derive a figure I say so.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation: what is new since the first and second sets, my changes to the forecast inputs, and the model's cross-check gaps

A. Five findings the earlier sets did not have (each is used in the answers)

A1. The fixed cost base has stopped growing, and the earlier sets did not see it. Operating costs other than materials (personnel plus other operating expenses, less other operating income) were 104.8, 104.1, 106.5, 111.6 and 114.5 in the five half-years from H1 2024 to H1 2026 (my derivation from the financials block: gross profit plus depreciation less EBITDA; FactSet's cost of sales here is materials plus depreciation, which I checked against the company's material ratios for 2024 and 2025). The last two include the businesses acquired in August 2025, whose non-material costs I estimate at about 7 and about 8.5. Excluding them, the cost base has been about 104 to 107 in every one of the five half-years, while half-year sales ranged from 135 to 199. Headcount fell 3.5% to 1,694 during H1 2026 from 1,755 (half-year report, as cited in two of the AlphaSense reports) while sales grew 22%. The second set's estimate that organic costs grew 6% to 8% in H1 2026 was taken from total operating expenses (+13.8%), which include materials that vary with volume; excluding materials and the acquired businesses, costs were about flat. The first set's description of the operating leverage ("shallow upside leverage by management's choice", hiring ahead of growth) describes the previous chief executive's behaviour in 2023 and 2024; the revealed behaviour since then is the opposite.

A2. "Consensus" is at least two models, and they disagree. The FactSet figures differ from UBS's own in every line (2026: sales 396, EBITDA 55.9, EPS 1.40, against UBS 399, 57.8, 1.54), so at least one unnamed broker is inside them; the second set's "one model" is not right. If there is exactly one other, its figures by arithmetic are: 2026 sales 393, EBITDA 54.0, EPS 1.26; 2027 460, 69.0, 1.80; 2028 542, 87.9, 2.38; and it alone supplies 2029 (632, 109, 3.08). Two consequences. First, the two contributors are about 20% apart on 2026 EPS with five weeks of the year left, which shows how little the sell-side knows about the second half. Second, the wide gap between our forecasts and "consensus" in 2028 and 2029 is mostly a gap to one unnamed model that grows sales 17% to 18% a year (the mid-term target extrapolated); against UBS, our 2028 sales are 4.7% lower, not 7.8%.

A3. On our own earnings the share is not cheap against its history. The 24-month-forward P/E of 29.6x (32nd percentile of five years; the dashboard's valuation rating B) rests on FactSet EPS of about 2.37 for the twelve months to November 2028. Our attributable EPS for 2028 is 2.02 (after my input changes below), which puts the share at about 34x to 35x at CHF 68.80 to 70.10: above the ten-year median of 32.7x and at the guideline fair multiple of 34.8x. Put another way, our attributable EPS for 2029 (2.33) is no higher than the EPS the market capitalises today (about 2.37). If estimates converge on our numbers, the two-year-forward EPS under the share at the end of 2027 will be the same as today's, and any price gain will have to come from the market paying a higher multiple on falling estimates.

A4. The first hard test may come on 28 January 2027, not 23 March. The AlphaSense report on the chief financial officer's appeal, citing SKAN's release of 17-Nov-26, gives 28 January 2027 for the publication of preliminary key figures for 2026 (it refers to order intake being disclosed then) and 28 April 2027 for the annual general meeting. Neither date is in the first set's sources, and the first and second sets both treat 23 March 2027 as the first sight of the second half. The memo should verify the dates (my first research request). If they are right, 2026 sales and second-half order intake will be known before Danaher, Thermo Fisher, Repligen and Sartorius report.

A5. No profit warning has been issued by 25 November. In 2025 the warning came on 6 November. Silence at this date lowers the probability of a 2025-type miss for 2026. It does not exclude a 2024-type slippage at the year-end, which was disclosed only with the results.

B. My changes to the forecast inputs (the lines are in inputs-changed.md)

I change four lines (the EBITDA margin, with the matching half-year margins; depreciation and amortisation; the dividend per share; working capital) and leave revenue, net financial items, tax, capital spending and everything else as the second set decided.

FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030

EBITDA margin: second set 14.1% 15.5% 16.5% 17.0% 17.3%
EBITDA margin: mine 14.5% 16.0% 16.5% 17.0% 17.3%

Half-year EBITDA margins: H2 2026 18.4% (second set 17.6%); H1 2027 12.6% (12.0%); H2 2027 18.6% (18.1%). Half-year revenue growth is unchanged (12.0%, 14.0%, 11.8%).

  • EBITDA margin, 2026 and 2027. Three reasons. (1) The second set's own segment table for 2026 (E&S 23.4 plus S&C 32.3) sums to 55.7, or 14.4% of sales, and its second-half segment margins (E&S 15.0% on 140.5, S&C 23.5% on 82.0) give 18.1%, not the 17.6% and 14.1% entered as inputs. (2) Finding A1: on a flat cost base, a second half of 222.5 of sales gives an EBITDA margin of roughly 17% to 22% depending on the material ratio (my arithmetic: non-material costs of 116 to 119, materials at 26% to 29% of sales, against 24.9% in H2 2025 and 29.2% in H2 2024). My 18.4% sits in the lower half of that range, to allow for PAS start-up costs, bonus accruals in a recovery year, a possible provision on the backlog item at risk of cancellation, and the higher material content of large integrated lines. (3) SKAN finished above the top of its 13% to 15% range in both of the years in which volume arrived (15.7% in 2023, 15.8% in 2024). For 2027 I carry the same half-point forward: the second set's 15.5% needs non-material costs to grow by about 10% in one year, which is at odds with five flat half-years and a falling headcount. I leave 2028 to 2030 unchanged, because the second set's path there already has costs growing more slowly than revenue and I have no evidence for those years; the effect is that the recovery in margin is front-loaded into 2026 and 2027 and slows as PAS, the United States build-out and renewed hiring add fixed cost.
  • Depreciation and amortisation, 2027 to 2030. The second set said the PAS charge "could be CHF 6m to 7m" rather than 5 if the premises are leased. The AlphaSense reports show that they are (rental and energy expense rose to 10.96 in 2025 from 9.73, and the half-year report attributes higher rent to the Allschwil expansion for Maeven), so more of the CHF 70m is short-lived fit-out and machinery. My figures match what the brokers already assume (EBITDA less EBIT in the FactSet figures: 18.4 in 2027, 21.0 in 2028).
  • Dividend per share. 30% of attributable EPS on my changed earnings: 0.41, 0.52, 0.61, 0.70, 0.82.
  • Working capital. See C below.

The net effect on attributable EPS is small (+4% in 2026, +3% in 2027, -2% to -3% in 2028 to 2030): the higher margin and the higher depreciation roughly offset. What changes is the shape. Our 2026 and 2027 EBITDA is now level with the FactSet figures (56.1 against 55.9; 69.8 against 70.1) on lower sales and a higher margin; our difference from the sell-side in the next 18 months is no longer about profit but about its composition; the real difference sits in 2028 and 2029.

C. The model's cross-check gaps, explained

  • EPS (every year: model 1.38, 1.76, 2.16, 2.47, 2.81 against the cross-check's 1.32, 1.69, 2.08, 2.38, 2.78). The whole gap is minority interests (1.2, 1.4, 1.6, 1.8 and 0.5, or CHF 0.05 to 0.08 a share, 0.02 in 2030), which the model does not deduct (Richard's ruling of 16-Oct-26) and the cross-check does. No input is wrong. But the Consumer must know which basis to use: brokers' EPS is after minorities (2025: brokers' 0.720 against reported attributable 0.724), so the model's own comparison with consensus (-1.8%, -4.5%, -9.9% for 2026 to 2028) understates the like-for-like gap by about four points, and the model's value per share at the fair multiple is about 3% too high for the same reason.
  • Free cash flow, FY2026 (flagged: cross-check 14.0, model 18.5). The model's 18.5 is a faithful reading of the inputs: EBITDA 54.6, less tax 6.6, less net financial items 3.5, plus a working-capital inflow of 4.8 (the balance falling from 128.3 to 31.9% of 387.0), less capital spending 30 and leases 1. The cross-check's 14.0 uses an operating cash flow of "about 45", which is about 4 lower than those same inputs produce. So the two halves of the second set's own work disagree, and the cross-check is the better figure. The reason is the second half: the second set assumes order intake of about 200 against sales of 222.5, so the backlog falls from 442 to about 420 and customer advances (about 30% of backlog, by the second set's own rule) should fall by about 6 in the second half, after rising 8.4 in the first. A full-year working-capital inflow of 4.4 to 4.8 is therefore too generous; about nil is right. The working-capital input is the wrong one, and I change it: 33.2% of revenue at the end of 2026 (a balance of 128.5, flat on 2025, and inside the 33.0% to 38.5% range of the last three year-ends on FactSet's basis, where the second set's 31.9% was below all of them). I shift the later years by the same amount so that their year-on-year cash effects stay as the second set intended (inflows of about 3.8, -1.1, 0.5 and 3.2 in 2027 to 2030): 28.6%, 26.0%, 23.8%, 21.2%. With my margin change, FY2026 free cash flow comes to about 14.6.
  • Free cash flow, 2027 to 2030 (model about 2 above the cross-check each year). The cross-check's operating cash flows are rounded ("about 59, 65, 75, 87") and sit about 2 below what the inputs give (60.9, 67.0, 76.9, 88.8). No input is wrong; the model is the consistent reading. Treat the 2 as an allowance for small cash items outside the input lines, such as dividends paid to minority shareholders.

  • How to read the model's net debt line. Two mechanics, not inputs, make it higher than the economic figure. (1) The model deducts each year's declared dividend in that same year (I can reproduce every year's change in net debt only on that basis: for example 26.0 of free cash flow less 11.5 of dividends, 3 of acquisitions and 1 of buybacks gives the 10.5 fall in 2027), although SKAN pays in the following spring; in FY2026 it therefore deducts both the 4.95 actually paid in H1 2026 and the 9.0 for 2026. (2) In the reported first half the model's cash-flow lines, built on FactSet's working-capital definition (which fell by 10.3 without a matching cash inflow), imply free cash flow of about 8 against the company's reported 2.7; this is most of the 7.7 "difference in the reported periods" on the summary page. I have set the working-capital input on the model's existing mechanics (year-end balances on FactSet's basis), so the full-year free cash flow comes out right without any change to the reported-period lines; the cost is that about 6 of the first-half difference stays in the net debt line. My estimate of economic net debt is about 30 at the end of 2026, about 16 at the end of 2027, about nil at the end of 2028 and of 2029 (after about 25 for the Metronik minority in 2029) and net cash of about 33 at the end of 2030. The model's line will read about 15 to 25 higher than these (about 6 higher if the dividend timing is corrected). The same timing puts 0.91 of dividends into the model's 18-month return where an investor will receive only the spring 2027 payment of about 0.41, so the dividend part of the 18-month return is about 0.6%, not 1.3%.

  • What the model's return becomes. The model's 18-month value of CHF 86.89 (+26.3%) is 34.8 times its FY2029 EPS of 2.47, plus 0.91 of dividends. On my inputs, on attributable EPS (2.33) and with dividends dated when paid, the same guideline multiple gives about CHF 81.5: +18% on CHF 68.80, +16% on CHF 70.10. The updated model is the record; this is my estimate of the direction.

The Financial Analyst should check whether the model deducts each fiscal year's declared dividend in that same year rather than in the following spring when it is paid, which would also count two dividends in FY2026 (the 0.22 paid in H1 2026 and the 0.40 declared for 2026) and would explain most of the difference between the model's net debt and the second set's.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of SKAN-CH with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-24 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH), a Swiss maker of isolators and aseptic process systems for sterile drug manufacturing, reporting two segments: Equipment & Solutions (custom and standard isolators, integrated fill-finish process systems built with partner filling-machine makers, eBeam and H2O2 decontamination, laboratory, sterility-testing and cell-therapy isolators) and Services & Consumables (requalification, maintenance, retrofits and spare parts for the installed base; Aseptic Technologies closed vials; ABC Transfer sterile transfer bags and ports; Metronik manufacturing execution software; and the Pre-Approved Services pay-per-use filling business run through Maeven AG).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What has SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH) disclosed and said about its cost base, headcount, second-half profitability and cash flow, and what is its reporting calendar for the next six months? Specifically: (1) headcount at each half-year and year-end from 2022 to 30 June 2026, in total and by country, segment and function, including the fall to 1,694 at 30 June 2026 from 1,755 at the end of 2025 reported in the Half-Year Report 2026 (17 Aug 2026): which roles and sites it came from, whether it was attrition, temporary staff or redundancies, and whether hiring has resumed; (2) personnel expenses, other operating expenses, other operating income, own work capitalised, the change in inventories and work in progress, and the material ratio, for each half-year from H1 2023 to H1 2026, and the cost base of the businesses acquired in 2025 (Metronik and ABC Transfer) within those lines; (3) variable pay and bonus accruals in 2025 and 2026 (no bonus was paid to management for 2025) and the share-based plans introduced in 2026; (4) what management meant by the "targeted measures to improve the cost base" in the Half-Year Report 2026 and by "operational excellence" and segment accountability, and whether any productivity, pricing or savings programme has been quantified or announced; (5) what chief executive Jonas Greutert did on costs, pricing, headcount and margins as head of Mettler-Toledo's Product Inspection division from 2020 to 2025, and what that division's results were; (6) what management said on the 18 Aug 2026 call and since about the second-half 2026 margin by segment, cost phasing, Pre-Approved Services start-up costs, capital spending for 2026 and 2027 (the chief financial officer referred on 24 Mar 2026 to capital spending at mid-single-digit levels), customer advances (CHF 132.2m at 30 June 2026), net working capital and free cash flow at year-end; (7) any SKAN or Swissmedic announcement since 18 Aug 2026 on the GMP certification of the Pre-Approved Services facility of Maeven AG, and whether its premises are leased or owned, when its depreciation starts and over what asset lives; and (8) SKAN's financial calendar: whether preliminary key figures for 2026 will be published on 28 January 2027, what SKAN's January releases contained in earlier years (sales, order intake, backlog, any margin indication), and the dates of the full-year 2026 results (23 March 2027) and the 2027 annual general meeting (28 April 2027). Quote the company's own words with dates, give figures in tables by half-year, and separate reported figures from inference.

Request: OWN-2

Question: What do the brokers that publish research on SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH) forecast and argue, and what do investors and the Swiss financial press say about the company? Identify every house with an analyst on SKAN itself (UBS, Baader Helvea, Berenberg, Zürcher Kantonalbank, Research Partners, Vontobel and Kepler Cheuvreux have been named; add any others found, and do not count brokers that write only on peers such as Stevanato, Getinge, Sartorius or Tecan). From each house's own notes published since SKAN's H1 2026 results of 18 Aug 2026, give: rating, target price, valuation method and the multiple applied; forecasts for 2026 to 2029 for sales by segment, EBITDA and margin by segment, Pre-Approved Services revenue and start date, depreciation, net financial result, tax rate, minority interests, EPS, capital spending, operating and free cash flow (UBS on 29 Sep 2026 had 2026 sales of CHF 399m, EBITDA of CHF 57.8m and EPS of CHF 1.54; 2027 sales of CHF 448m, EBITDA of CHF 71.2m and EPS of CHF 1.88; 2028 sales of CHF 508m, EBITDA of CHF 86.5m and EPS of CHF 2.40); how each changed its estimates and target price over the last 12 months and why; and what each says, if anything, about: delivery in the second half of 2026 and the 2027 guidance expected with the results on 23 March 2027; the Services & Consumables margin and its organic level excluding acquisitions (a question raised by the analyst Hans Alig); headcount and cost discipline under the chief executive appointed on 1 Jan 2026; order intake in the second half of 2026 and in 2027, United States onshoring and the EU GMP Annex 1 upgrade cycle; a plateau in pharmaceutical capital spending after 2027; turnkey competition from Syntegon, Optima and other filling-machine makers with their own isolators; the timing, pricing and customer uptake of Pre-Approved Services; customer advances and cash conversion; the offsetting of goodwill against equity under Swiss GAAP FER; and the chief financial officer's first-instance conviction of 17 Nov 2026. State which of these topics no broker discusses. Separately, report investor and press commentary with dates: fund manager letters and fact sheets that name SKAN (for example zCapital's Swiss ESG fund report of 30 Sep 2026), disclosed changes in significant shareholdings since January 2026, short interest, coverage in Finanz und Wirtschaft, The Market, Neue Zürcher Zeitung, AWP and Cash, and commentary on X (Twitter) or investor forums. State how many covering houses were found and for how many the house's own research was read; credit a view to a house only when citing that house's own document.

Request: OWN-3

Question: How have the share prices and valuation multiples of SKAN Group AG (SKAN-CH) and of comparable listed life-science, medical-technology and pharmaceutical-equipment companies behaved in the 6 to 24 months after a recovery rally that was led by the multiple and not by earnings, and what decided the outcome? SKAN's share price rose about 48% in the twelve months to 23 Nov 2026 while the FactSet consensus EPS for 2027 fell about 30% over the same twelve months, its mean broker target price rose 15% in six months, and its five-year range of 24-month-forward P/E is 17.9x to 64.0x (FactSet). For SKAN's own history since its listing in October 2021, set out what orders, guidance and consensus estimates were doing at each high and low of the share price and of the P/E, including the period around the profit warning of 6 Nov 2025 and the recovery after it. For comparable cases, including Sartorius and Sartorius Stedim Biotech (2023 to 2026), Tecan (2024 to 2026, including its share price reaction to the H1 2026 results), Stevanato Group (2024 to 2026, including the reaction to its 2027 outlook of 3 Nov 2026), Getinge, GEA Group, Chemometec, Stratec, Bachem, Siegfried, Ypsomed, Lonza, Repligen and other European and United States tools and equipment makers with lumpy orders, give for each: the size and duration of the rally, how much of it was multiple and how much earnings, what consensus estimates then did, whether the company met, raised or cut guidance at the next two to four reporting dates, what happened to the share price and the forward P/E in each case, and what multiples brokers used in their target prices at each stage. Distinguish cases in which estimates later rose, stayed flat or fell; cases with a new chief executive giving a first full-year guide; cases with thin analyst coverage and low trading liquidity; and cases with a governance overhang such as a senior executive under legal proceedings. Also report what brokers and strategists currently say about investor positioning and flows in European healthcare and medical-technology small and mid caps and how long comparable sector rotations lasted. Be comprehensive: cover every named company and any other relevant precedent, date every piece of evidence, separate what the evidence shows from inference, and conclude with the patterns the evidence supports and the indicators that distinguished rallies that were sustained from those that reversed.
